The two halves are not equally strong and the code says so: worktrees are an **invariant** (`registeredWorktreeNames`, read inside the function — store-only, no system reached and no filtering by what git currently reports, so neither a caller nor a system being down can change its answer), while live instances are a **caller contract** (`src/instances.ts` imports this module, so the manager must be injected; a required non-defaultable getter raises the bar, but a caller supplying `() => []` still defeats it). The worktree half is what makes `WorktreeMeta`'s deliberate absence of a stored target safe — a worktree re-derives `(system, remoteId, path)` from its parent on every operation, and the parent cannot move while a registration exists. On a permitted change it verifies the new target through `systemById` BEFORE persisting, writes the record and invalidates `projectsCache` — it removes NOTHING local, because there is no cc-owned copy of the old target to go stale. `projectsBySystem()` groups `{name, remoteId}` by the non-local system their record names — the target comes with the name because one row can serve many, and the name alone would not say which holds it — (the still-referenced check behind Settings → Systems' delete refusal); it reads `listProjects`, so a store directory left behind by a deleted project is invisible: without a record it is not a project at all. An adopted project's stored path is the target's **REALPATH** — load-bearing, not tidiness: the Claude CLI encodes `~/.claude/projects/<encoded-cwd>/` from `getcwd()` (always the realpath) and walks `CLAUDE.md` upward along the realpath, neither with an env lever, so a logical-path project would strand every resume. `adoptProject(name, target, {system?, remoteId?, onStaleRecord?})` writes the record after every validation (soft refusals returned with a `code`, never thrown) then delivers `ensureProjectConventionsMd` non-fatally. **NESTING UNDER THE PROJECTS ROOT IS LEGAL** — the old "target is inside the root" refusal is gone, and it is what makes a container directory work; what stays refused is a target that CONTAINS the root (`TARGET_ALREADY_MANAGED`) and one inside cc's own state — `orchStoreRoot()`, `localWorktreesRoot()` or `pluginsRoot()` (`TARGET_IS_CC_STATE`; `.plugins` is not optional there, because the Library's post-clone step is best-effort and a half-installed checkout can persist under it). The duplicate test compares **(system, remoteId, path)** for BOTH kinds — two targets each hosting `/app` are two different trees. A held name whose record's path no longer resolves is **`PROJECT_EXISTS_STALE`** carrying `heldPath` + `discards`, and `onStaleRecord: 'relocate'` (repoint the record, keep the store subtree, invalidate `projectsCache` — the cached git facts were measured at the old path — and repair the project's worktrees in both directions: `git worktree repair` from the new tree for the gitdir back-references, and a `parentPath` rewrite for every root-based registration, both best-effort so a relocation that stands is never answered with a 500) or `'replace'` (drop the store subtree, register afresh) resolves it. A `relocate` is **refused `PROJECT_PLACEMENT_IN_USE`** while a project ON A SYSTEM has registered worktrees, the same shape and reason `setProjectRemote` refuses a target change: `worktreePathFor` derives a remote checkout's path from the project's, the checkout does not move with the tree, and stored-versus-derived would diverge permanently — which disarms the transcript guard, whose whole correctness is that it re-derives. A LOCAL relocate needs no such guard: cc's `.worktrees` root does not depend on where the project's tree is. Then: a system that could not ANSWER makes staleness undecidable and gets **`PROJECT_EXISTS_UNRESOLVABLE`** with no relocation offered. Naming a `system` runs the realpath, stat and `git rev-parse --show-toplevel` ON THAT SYSTEM — and so does the `--git-dir` probe, on the branch where `--show-toplevel` failed; together they decide only the enclosing-repo and no-work-tree questions, a plain non-git directory being adoptable. The local containment tests are SKIPPED for a remote adopt (a path on another machine neither is inside cc's root nor contains it, however the strings compare). `createProject(name, {conventionsDoc?, system?, remoteId?, systemPath?})` takes the same triple (`validatePlacementInput` is shared, and requires system+path both or neither, absolute; `remoteId` is optional even with a system and is refused without one, validated by `validateRemoteId` — deliberately NOT `isSlug`, since container names, hostnames and VM ids carry `_` and `.`): the `mkdir`, `git init` and seed files happen on the system, and the record is written only AFTER the mkdir succeeds, so a refused create adopts nothing that was already there. **`deleteProject(name, {deleteDirectory})` DEREGISTERS**: `removeProjectStoreDir` plus an `fs.rm` of `<projectsRoot>/.worktrees/<name>/`, and the TREE goes only when the caller ticks `deleteDirectory` — one behaviour for every project, whichever way it was registered. For a project on a system that tick is refused **400**: cc owns no area on another machine, and removing a directory there needs its own design. It deliberately does not resolve the system, and neither does the ROUTE in front of it — `getProjectForDelete` addresses the project by its record alone (and tolerates one it cannot parse, since deleting is the repair for exactly that state), because `getProject` resolves the system and putting it there made a project on a down system undeletable, deadlocking it against its own system row's 409. Returns `{name, path, system, remoteId, directoryDeleted}`. The route's cascade runs `removeAllWorktreesForProject` with **`force: false`** and PROPAGATES its refusals — a dirty, dirty-unknown or depended-on worktree 409s the whole delete rather than being force-removed; a checkout that is not there, or whose parent is no longer a repo, has nothing to protect and is dropped instead, which is what keeps a project whose tree vanished out-of-band deletable. A refusal raised AFTER `git worktree remove` succeeded (the best-effort branch delete) does not abort the cascade: whether the registration is still there is the state that says which happened, and it says so without matching on message text. A REMOTE project's worktrees are unregistered with no git run — that would delete a checkout and a branch inside the user's repo. Deleting ONE worktree still removes it: cc created that directory and the user asked for that directory. The cascade order (worktrees before the project) is load-bearing: deregistering first turns `listWorktrees` into a 404 and orphans both the checkouts and the registrations. `createProject()` runs `git init -q` in the new dir (unconditionally — the `mkdir` above it proves the dir is new; an `isGitRepo()` guard would be wrong, it walks UP and would skip the init whenever the projects root itself sits inside a repo) before seeding any file, so a failing init leaves an empty dir and throws 500 rather than a seeded non-repo; `runGit` comes from a lazy `await import('./worktrees.ts')`, since worktrees.ts statically imports this module. It then ends at `commitScaffold(system, full, runGit, scaffolded)` — `git add -A` (no pathspec, so the commit stays forwards-compatible with whatever a future scaffold writes), then `git add -f --` the paths creation actually wrote (`scaffolded`, appended at each write site rather than listed up front), then `git commit -m SCAFFOLD_COMMIT_MESSAGE` — which is what makes a brand-new project worktree-ready without a human commit. `-A` decides the scope and `-f` guarantees the floor: `-A` alone honours the user's `core.excludesFile`, so a global ignore listing `CLAUDE.md` would drop it from the commit and a worktree branched off that HEAD would check out no `CLAUDE.md`, losing the `@CONVENTIONS.md` import chain for every worker in the project. **The commit is preceded by a repo-identity precondition**, established before anything is staged: `git rev-parse --absolute-git-dir --show-toplevel` must answer with `<realpath(full)>/.git` and `realpath(full)` — the realpath because git resolves symlinks in both answers, and both halves because the git dir says which repository the commit lands in while the top level says which working tree `add -A` sweeps. An ambient `GIT_DIR` already sends the `git init` to a foreign repo (the pre-existing dent pinned by `createProject fails loudly when git init fails`); without this check the staging then wrote a stray `Initial commit` onto that repo's branch carrying a tree that DELETED every file it had tracked — measured, and the reason target identity is a precondition here rather than something noticed afterwards. Identity is probed per field and `SCAFFOLD_AUTHOR_NAME`/`SCAFFOLD_AUTHOR_EMAIL` injected as leading `-c` pairs in the argv only for whichever of `user.name`/`user.email` is unset — never `opts.env` (a frame `env` replaces the far side's environment) and never a `git config` write, so the fallback outlives nothing; `runGit`'s `sub` extraction already skips `-c` values, so a refusal still names `commit`. The whole block is non-fatal: its `try` wraps the `runGit` CALLS (they throw `GIT_TIMED_OUT`/`GIT_DID_NOT_RUN` rather than returning a code), and any failure is a `console.warn` leaving creation successful and HEAD unborn — throwing would 500 over a project whose directory, record, repo and seed files all already exist. No `--no-verify` and no gpgsign override. `adoptProject` gains nothing from this: it never inits and never commits, so an adopted repo's history stays the user's. `findSessionLocation(sessionId)` probes every cwd every registered project + worktree ADMITS and returns `{project, worktreeName, cwd}` — **`cwd` REQUIRED**, being the directory the transcript was actually found in, because a caller that re-derives it from the project tree looks on the OTHER MACHINE for a project on a system and reads an empty transcript; an optional field would invite `hit.cwd ?? proj.path`, which is that bug. It sweeps ONE place list ONCE: each place's cwd, which is its tree path on whatever machine it lives on — `worktreePathFor` for a worktree, the registered path for a project. There is no second pass and no local-vs-remote split: a place admits exactly ONE candidate cwd. A SET of candidates — with a raw remote path only ever a demoted fallback — is what a remote session running in a cc-owned local image would need, and there is no such image. 
- **src/sessionActivity.ts** — `lastActivityOf(path, stat)`: when a session transcript was last ACTUALLY active, as epoch ms. The ISO `timestamp` on the LAST timestamped record in the jsonl (last in file order, not newest by value — records are appended in real time, so the two differ only where interleaved sidechain records land a few ms out of order), found by a bounded 64 KB tail read; falls back to `stat.mtimeMs` when the tail holds no timestamped record. **Not mtime**, because the CLI appends untimestamped bookkeeping records (`last-prompt`, `mode`, `ai-title`) as its process exits — so a mass subprocess exit (a server drain/restart) stamps every live session's file within the same millisecond, collapsing distinct sessions into one indistinguishable minute days after they actually stopped. Feeds `SessionRow.lastActivity`, `ArchivedSessionRow.lastActivity` and `summarizeSessions().lastActivity`, so every surface reports one definition of recency. Results are memoized in a process-lifetime LRU (`createLastActivityCache({max})`, cap `LAST_ACTIVITY_CACHE_MAX`) keyed on the path with a `(dev, ino, ctimeMs, mtimeMs, size)` validity tuple. `ctimeMs` is what makes it sound — it cannot be rolled back from userspace, so it catches even an in-place rewrite that restores mtime and preserves size; `mtimeMs`/`size` catch the common case (an append) without depending on ctime semantics. `dev`/`ino` are **redundant** against every writer in this repo (the two that replace a transcript go tmp→rename, and a rename-over sets a fresh ctime) and are kept only as a guard against a future writer that replaces a file without moving ctime — no test isolates them, because with ctime in the key the case isn't constructible. No stale entry is reachable, so there is no TTL. **In-memory only, never persisted.** Both callers already stat every file, so a steady-state fan-out across every project costs readdir + stat + a Map lookup — the property the "just readdir + stat, no opens" walk in `summarizeSessions` was protecting.
- **src/sessionModes.ts** — the mode vocabulary (`MODES`, `DEFAULT_MODE`, `DEFAULT_RESUME_MODE`) plus a sidecar store (`<store>/session-modes.json`) of the mode each session was last running in. `loadAll()`/`getSessionMode(sid)`/`markSessionMode(sid, mode)`/`unmarkSessionMode(sid)`, same atomic-write + lock shape as `conductedSessions.ts`. Written from `Instance._recordMode` (spawn, `system/init` sync, `setMode`), plus the explicit carry in `carryMarkersAcrossRenewal`. That carry exists for exactly one case: **`ask`**. For `plan`/`bypassPermissions` it is redundant — the `system/init` handler rotates the backing id and records the CLI-reported mode in the same breath, and the fork path relaunches so `spawn()` records there — but `ask` is orchestrator-only, the CLI reports the rotated session as `bypassPermissions`, and the handler's anti-clobber guard skips the record write along with the assignment. Without the carry an `ask` worker's rotated id stays unrecorded and so resolves to `DEFAULT_RESUME_MODE`: listed `resumes-hot` and resumed ungated. Pinned by *renew_session carries the mode record for an `ask` session* in `tests/renew-session.test.mjs`. The old sessionId **keeps** its record (unlike its temp marker, which is cleaned): it survives as an archived, still-listable row whose `resumes-hot` flag has to stay accurate. Same treatment as the `conducted` and `title` markers carried in that function. Two resolvers own the read side: `effectiveResumeMode(recorded)` → `recorded ?? DEFAULT_RESUME_MODE`, and `resumesHot(mode)` → `mode === 'bypassPermissions'`. **There is no backfill and no migration** — a session predating the store is unrecorded and resolves to `DEFAULT_RESUME_MODE`, i.e. the behaviour it had before. Because of that, everything reporting on a resume reports the EFFECTIVE mode: `SessionRow.resumeMode` and the `resumes-hot` flag both derive from it, so an unrecorded session is shown as hot rather than as unknown. It also owns `markerPermissionMode(mode)` — how a mode is RECORDED in the CLI's own jsonl, which is a different question from `cliPermissionMode` (instances.ts) and must not share its mapping. The CLI's vocabulary (`dontAsk|auto|default|acceptEdits|plan|bypassPermissions`) has no `ask`, so `ask` is recorded as `default` — the CLI mode that prompts. The marker is still deliberately **not** an input to this store: `default` is not a mode the orchestrator can spawn in, so `ask` cannot round-trip through the jsonl either way. `writeSessionMetadata` (transcript.ts) takes the ORCHESTRATOR mode and applies the mapping itself, so no call site can record a live-wire value; `cliPermissionMode` survives at exactly two sites, the `--permission-mode` argv and the `set_permission_mode` control request.
- **src/conductedSessions.ts** — sidecar store (`<store>/conducted-sessions.json`) of sessionIds spawned via MCP `spawn_instance` (the worker agents an orchestrator conducts). `loadAll()`/`isConducted(sid)`/`markConducted(sid)`/`unmarkConducted(sid)`, on the shared `jsonStore.ts` machinery. The durable half of the conducted axis.
- **session sidecar stores** — sibling `<store>` JSON sidecars, all six built on `createJsonStore` (`jsonStore.ts`) so the lock + write-chain + atomic write is written once. (Copy-pasted per store is how `sessionTitles.ts`/`sessionSummaries.ts` drifted WITHOUT the lock, opening the lost-update class described below for titles and summaries; on `createJsonStore` it is closed for all six, and `tests/store-lock-lost-update.test.mjs` is the regression guard.) The stores: `sessionTitles.ts` (`{sessionId: customLabel}` — `getTitle`/`setTitle`/`deleteTitle`), `sessionBackends.ts` (Map `{sid: {backend, model|null, contextWindowTokens|null}}` of sessions on a substitution backend — `hasSessionBackend`/`getSessionBackend`/`markSessionBackend(sid,backend,model,contextWindowTokens)`/`unmarkSessionBackend`; the durable marker that survives every resume path — written at spawn, read in `_doCreate` on resume to recover the `backend` id **and** the exact model id, carried to the new sid in `carryMarkersAcrossRenewal`. It carries the model id IN FULL because the inner CLI records `message.model` lossily in the jsonl (`:tag` and any terminal `[…]` build tag dropped), so the jsonl value can't rebuild the backend template's `--model <key>`; a `null` model is unknown (a legacy entry) and falls back to the jsonl, self-healing on the next mark. `contextWindowTokens` is the last known capacity and is a **fallback only** — live registry resolution wins whenever it succeeds, so it matters just when the model's custom-model row has since been deleted. Absence of a record means the identity `claude` backend. Claude sessions store nothing), `archivedSessions.ts` (archived-sid set — `isArchived`/`markArchived`/`unmarkArchived`), `tempSessions.ts` (durable temp-session markers that survive SIGKILL so a temp stays resumable across the resume-after-restart path). `storeLock.ts` provides the cross-process advisory lock (`withLock()`, `O_EXCL` create) they serialise on: a held lock is reclaimed **only when the owner PID is dead** — a live owner is never evicted on age, no matter how slow. This is load-bearing on Termux: post-restart CPU throttle (~10–75×) can starve a live holder for many seconds mid-write, and the old-server/new-server overlap during a restart is the only window with cross-process contention; the previous 10 s age-based eviction reclaimed the live holder there, letting two writers run the read-modify-write concurrently and drop each other's entries (a lost update) — which silently un-archived a subset of sessions on every restart. A slow holder is now waited out via bounded retries (a truly wedged holder throws on acquire rather than corrupting), and release is **ownership-token-checked** so a process can't delete a successor's lock. `archivedSessions.ts` additionally guards against a **silent full-reset** under the host's frequent OOM/low-disk churn: it keeps a rolling `.bak` (refreshed under the lock only from a non-empty write that is a **superset** of the current backup — never a shrink — so a spurious empty/tiny base can't canonize over the last-good snapshot; consequently `.bak` may lag behind un-archives by design, the safe direction: a recovery from a stale `.bak` only re-archives a few since-unarchived ids, never loses data); a missing primary recovers from `.bak` (missing primary **and** `.bak` = legitimately empty); a corrupt primary is quarantined to `.corrupt-<pid>-<ts>` (under the lock, in the mutation path) and recovered from `.bak` rather than bricking archiving; and a drain-to-empty now writes `{"sessions":[]}` atomically **instead of unlinking**, so an absent primary unambiguously means external loss. Only a genuine unrecoverable I/O error (EACCES etc.) still aborts a mutation. `tempCleanup.ts` writes/sweeps `<store>/pending-temp-cleanup.json` to GC temp jsonl orphaned after exit; it and `resumeManifest.ts` write their sync-before-exit manifests via tmp-write + `renameSync` so an OOM mid-write can't leave a torn manifest.
- **src/conduct.ts** — two entry points. `ensureConductProject()` lazy-creates `<projectsRoot>/.conduct/` (the cwd of every conductor session), guarantees its `CLAUDE.md` carries the `@CONVENTIONS.md` import via the shared `ensureConventionsImport` (`src/conventionsImport.ts`), and returns `{path, created}` (`created` = the dir was created). `materializeCurrentConduct()` calls that ensure, then writes `` `${await composeCurrentWorkspace()}\n${await composeCurrentConduct()}` `` to `conductConventionsPath()` = `<projectsRoot>/.conduct/CONVENTIONS.md` (`writeFileAtomic`, one fixed path) — the conductor is a delivery destination for **workspace** conventions too, and they go first (the workspace text ends with a newline, so exactly one blank line separates them and the role doc's generated footer stays last). It is called from `Instance.launch()` for `isConductorInstance()` instances only — before **every** spawn and resume, since every (re)launch entry point funnels through `launch()`, the sole caller of `spawn()`. A compose/write failure propagates (a role-less conductor is worse than a surfaced error). **Delivery is the messages stream, not an appended system prompt:** every non-Anthropic backend here is the same `claude` CLI behind a translation proxy, and a proxy that reads only `system[0]` or flattens `system` to a string drops the CLI's extra `system` block — leaving a conductor with no role doc at all. A CLAUDE.md `@`-import is a channel the backend already honours for every worker. **In the project rather than a store path `@`-imported from there:** an import resolving outside the project root is gated behind the external-includes dialog, which never fires headless (`migrations/0003`). **A partial revert of `migrations/0022`, whose two objections are answered:** *freshness* — the pre-spawn materializer is the SOLE writer of `CONVENTIONS.md`, so no ensure-time copy exists that could drift from the live selection (`ensureConductProject()` deliberately does not write it); *ownership* — that file is app-owned and says so in its own composed footer, while the user's `.conduct/CLAUDE.md` only ever gains an import line. This file is also the diagnostic artefact: it holds the actual delivered bytes, in the conductor's own cwd. `ensureConductProject()` is called (idempotently) on boot, on Conduct-button-tap `POST /api/projects/.conduct/ensure`, and on conductor resume-restart. **Selection changes affect newly-spawned / next-context-refresh conductor sessions, not an in-flight turn.**
- **src/conductorConventions.ts** — the toggleable conductor conventions composed into the conductor's role doc (written to `.conduct/CONVENTIONS.md` and reaching the session as a CLAUDE.md `@`-import; see `src/conduct.ts` above). **Single-slot channel:** the conductor has exactly **one** delivery document, and the CLI's `--append-system-prompt` / `--append-system-prompt-file` flags are deliberately unused (a translation proxy can drop the extra `system` block they produce), so any future feature appending system-prompt text must compose into this same document rather than reaching for a flag. Always-on `conventions/conductor/core.md` (role, hard boundary, dispatch-and-wake, MCP toolbelt, project-conventions-on-creation, safety, talking to the user) + `conventions/conductor/footer.md` bracket the enabled conventions. `SEED_CONVENTIONS` (the built-in `{slug,name,description}` entries; bodies in `conventions/conductor/<slug>.md`) + custom conventions + selection state persist at `<store>/conventions/conductor.json` (`{disabled:[slugs], rules:[…]}`). `disabled` is a **deny-list** of the user's explicit off-switches — seed, custom and namespaced `<id>/<slug>` plugin slugs alike, in one list; everything else the scope knows is on, so a fresh install matches the pre-carve CONDUCT.md **and** a future-added seed defaults on with no migration (see `src/conventionSelection.ts` below). A plugin's conductor conventions are **on by default** whenever the plugin is enabled. The **effective selection** (`getSelection`) is derived: base ∪ (conventions of currently-enabled plugins − `disabled`) — plugin membership comes from the live catalog (`getCatalog` surfaces ENABLED plugins' conventions only), so a disabled plugin's conventions vanish automatically, a plugin update adding/removing a convention flows through with no toggle, and a stale slug can never reach `compose()` (no 400). `getSelection`/`setSelection`/`deleteCustomConvention` come from the shared `src/conventionSelection.ts` collaborator; this scope supplies exactly one of its hooks — `derive` (fold in the enabled plugins' conventions minus `disabled`). Also `add/updateCustomConvention`, `composeConduct(slugs)`/`composeCurrentConduct()`. **A degraded catalog composes anyway, and logs one line**: `composeConduct` takes the flag off the catalog read the compose already makes (`fragmentCatalog`'s `composeWithMeta(slugs)` → `{text, degraded}`; `compose` is now `(await composeWithMeta(slugs)).text`) and, when it is up, emits exactly one `console.warn` — `conductorConventions: role doc composed over a DEGRADED convention catalog — a plugin's conductor conventions may be missing from this document`. The document itself is never marked and the write is never skipped, so a degraded role doc is byte-identical to one composed with that plugin deliberately disabled. It deliberately does NOT adopt `ensureProjectConventionsMd`'s freeze: that freeze is keyed on the `missing` set a project's persisted line-1 `<!-- cc:conventions … -->` marker yields, and this scope has no such record — the plugin half of the selection is derived live and an ENABLED one appears nowhere in `conductor.json` even after an explicit Save — so a freeze here could key only on `degraded` alone, which also fires when the conductor scope lost nothing (hence the wording's `may`). The read sits ABOVE the playbooks-gated generated sections, or the signal would vanish whenever that convention is off. Built on the shared `fragmentCatalog.ts` (`extraProvider` wired to enabled plugins' `conductor`-scope conventions via `setPluginConductorConventionsProvider`/`server.ts`, mirroring `projectConventions.ts`'s provider seam). Plugin enable/disable needs no regeneration step — the effective selection derives from the live catalog, so a toggled plugin's conventions flow into the composed doc on the next conductor spawn/resume. Imported by `src/routes.ts` and `src/mcp/handlers.ts` for the selection/catalog surface; `composeCurrentConduct` itself has exactly one caller, `materializeCurrentConduct` in `src/conduct.ts` (called from `Instance.launch()` in `src/instances.ts` for conductor instances only).
- **src/conventionSelection.ts** — the shared SELECTION collaborator sitting on top of a `FragmentCatalog`: which of its entries are enabled, and what a submitted checkbox set persists. `createSelectionStore({catalog, seeds, noun, derive?})` → `getSelection`/`setSelection`/`deleteCustom`; `disabledOf(state)` → `Set<string>` is exported for the conductor scope's `derive`. **The persisted state is a DENY-list `state.disabled`, never an allow-list**: a slug added to a `SEED_CONVENTIONS` array reaches every install, fresh or existing, with no migration — the allow-list it replaced needed one per added seed (`migrations/0015-…`, `migrations/0029-…`) because any store that had ever saved a selection froze the set it was written with. Owns, once: the base read (`seeds ∪ catalog.customSlugsOf(state) − state.disabled`, in seed-then-custom order — so the effective selection never echoes the order a save submitted, and the composed document's section order is a property of `SEED_CONVENTIONS` alone), `setSelection`'s array guard (`400 'enabled must be an array of slug strings'`) and unknown-slug loop (`400 unknown <noun> slug '<slug>'`), the submitted-set → `disabled` diff (for every slug the LIVE catalog can see: checked clears its off-switch, unchecked records one — a slug the catalog cannot see, e.g. an unreachable or disabled plugin's convention, keeps its off-state, so an off-switch survives disable→re-enable), and the delete-prunes-it-from-`disabled` rule (so a re-created custom of the same slug is not silently OFF). The two 400 messages and the `setSelection(enabled)` parameter still say `enabled`: the **wire** payload is an allow-list of what the user ticked (`{enabled:[…]}`, unchanged — see [protocol.md](protocol.md)), and only the persisted representation is inverted. Two scopes use it: **workspace** with no hooks, **conductor** with only `derive` (fold in the enabled plugins' conventions minus `disabled`; no `persist` override, because one deny-list holds plugin off-switches too). The **project** scope opts out entirely — it has no selection state, its picks living in the in-tree `CONVENTIONS.md` line-1 marker (`src/projectClaudeMd.ts`). `derive` gets the catalog as a **lazy thunk**, not a resolved list: workspace's derivation is store-read-only, and an eager fetch would make every `composeCurrentWorkspace()` / `GET /settings/conventions/workspace` re-read all seed fragments off disk. That is also why `customSlugsOf` is a pure projection over already-read state on `fragmentCatalog.ts` rather than a catalog read — `getSelection` makes exactly one store read and resolves no fragment. **A newly created custom convention is therefore ON immediately** (nothing switches it off), matching seeds and plugin conventions; under the allow-list it sat unchecked until the next save.
- **src/fragmentCatalog.ts** — shared helper backing all three convention systems (`projectConventions.ts`, `conductorConventions.ts`, `workspaceConventions.ts`): a catalog of `{slug,name,description}` seed metadata whose body loads from a committed `.md` fragment (`<seedDir>/<slug>.md`), plus custom entries whose body lives inline in a JSON store under `<store>`. **Seed bodies are read per call and never memoised**, and so are the always-on `core.md`/`footer.md` reads in `workspaceConventions.ts`/`conductorConventions.ts`: every document these bodies feed is regenerated OVER a committed file (`.conduct/CONVENTIONS.md` before each conductor spawn/resume, each project's `CONVENTIONS.md` on boot and after every workspace-settings save), so a body frozen at first read does not merely age — the next regeneration writes pre-edit text back over the correct one and the edit silently reverts until the orchestrator restarts. The cost this buys off, measured over 1000 iterations with a warm page cache and the reads issued under `Promise.all` as `getCatalog()` issues them: **0.13 ms** per workspace composition (core + 6 seeds), **0.21 ms** per conductor composition (core + footer + 9 seeds), **0.43 ms** for all 22 fragments of the three scopes (42 KB total) — against a conductor spawn launching a `claude` subprocess, and a regeneration sweep doing one read + one write per project through that project's `System`. `tests/fragment-catalog.test.mjs` and `tests/fragment-freshness.test.mjs` pin it. `createFragmentCatalog({seeds, seedDir, storeFile, noun, extraProvider?})` → `getCatalog`/`addCustom`/`updateCustom`/`deleteCustom` (validation with `statusCode` 400/404/409, seeds reject mutation), `compose(slugs)` (`'\n'+bodies.join('\n\n')+'\n'`, unknown slug → 400), and `readState`/`patchState` (arbitrary sibling keys on the same store JSON, preserving `rules` — used by the conductor/workspace scopes for `enabled`). **`extraProvider`** is an optional `async () => [{slug,name,description,body,…}]` merged into `getCatalog()` after seeds+custom (`builtin:false`, read-only, bypasses the custom-store CRUD) — used by `projectConventions` and `conductorConventions` to fold in enabled plugins' convention fragments (slugs pre-namespaced by the provider), one per routed plugin-convention scope. A throwing provider degrades to no extra entries (warned). `getCatalog()` returns a `CatalogList` (`CatalogEntry[] & {degraded?:boolean}`) — `degraded` is set true when the provider threw, or when it returned successfully but flagged its own list incomplete (registry.ts's `conventions()` does this on a transient per-plugin resolution failure). A caller that treats "slug absent from the catalog" as "safe to drop" must check this first. **Three places act on it** — the regeneration freeze (`ensureProjectConventionsMd`, the `src/projectClaudeMd.ts` bullet below), the conductor role doc's operator warn (`composeConduct`, `src/conductorConventions.ts`), and the conventions LISTING + create path (`listProjectConventions`/`listConductorConventions`/`createProject`, `src/mcp/handlers.ts`) — and a fourth, `compose`/`composeWithMeta`, propagates it rather than deciding on it. **The flag does not survive projection or serialization**: `degraded` is the array's OWN property, so `.map()` drops it and so does `JSON.stringify` — which is why the two MCP listings return `{conventions, incomplete?}` objects rather than the bare array, and why `GET /api/settings/conventions/{project,conductor}` still drop it (`res.json` is a `JSON.stringify`; deferred by decision, with a comment at each site).
- **src/identifiers.ts** — `SLUG_RE`/`SLUG_MAX`/`isSlug` (lowercase kebab: convention + rule slugs, plugin ids, plugin convention/role slugs, backend registry ids) and `SESSION_ID_RE`/`isSessionId` (also the path-traversal guard, since sessionIds build `<encoded-cwd>/<sid>.jsonl` paths). The RULE is shared; the REFUSAL stays at each call site, which differ on purpose — `fragmentCatalog`/`appSettings` throw 400, `plugins/manifest` accumulates into `errors[]`, `routes` throws 400 while `projects` returns null. NOT the custom-role name rule (`^[A-Za-z][A-Za-z0-9-]*$`, `appSettings.ts`), which is a case-preserving user label rather than a machine identifier.
- **src/mcp/argValidation.ts** — `validateArgs(schema, args, toolName)` (shallow JSON-Schema check at tools/call: object schemas, required keys, unknown-property rejection, scalar types + constraint keywords; no `$ref`/`oneOf`/`anyOf`/`allOf`/`not`) plus the vocabulary it enforces: `VALIDATED_CONSTRAINT_KEYS`, `DESCRIPTIVE_PROP_KEYS`, and the derived `ALLOWED_PROP_KEYS`. Extracted from `mcp/server.ts` so the vocabulary lives with its enforcer and `plugins/manifest.ts`'s `checkSchemaSubset` can IMPORT the allow-list instead of re-encoding it by hand — a hand-encoded copy ships a constraint nothing checks the moment one side widens without the other. Imports nothing from `../plugins` (that edge would close a cycle). Pinned by `tests/schema-keyword-sync.test.mjs`, which drives every listed keyword through the real validator rather than asserting set equality.
- **src/groupedCommand.ts** — the local implementation of the System `exec` primitive (its option/result shapes ARE `ExecOptions`/`ExecResult` from `src/systems/system.ts`; its output accounting IS `ExecOutputCollector` from `src/systems/execCollector.ts`, shared with the wire implementation). `runGroupedCommand({argv}|{shell}, {cwd, env?, timeoutMs?, cap?, headCapBytes?, maxBufferBytes?, onChunk?, killGraceMs?, stdin?})` → `{code, stdout, stderr, output, timedOut, truncated, durationMs, spawnError}`, plus `killProcessGroup(pid, {graceMs, fallback})` and `GROUP_OUTPUT_CAP`. The one detached process-group runner. `detached: true` makes the child its own group leader so one `kill(-pid)` reaches every grandchild — a timed-out `npm ci` otherwise keeps running after the request that started it is gone — and a SIGTERM → unref'd SIGKILL backstop handles a script that traps SIGTERM. Never rejects: a spawn failure is `{code:1, spawnError}`, a timeout is `{code:124, timedOut:true}` (the `timeout(1)` convention callers already branch on). **RECORDED, NOT FIXED: it settles on `proc.on('close', …)` too, so it has the same misreport as the wire implementation did** — measured, `sleep 60 & echo started` through `LocalSystem.exec` returns `{code:124, timedOut:true}` after 3008 ms against controls of 15 ms and 26 ms, bounded by its own `timeoutMs` rather than left unbounded. It is out of scope here because it **cannot carry a redirected Bash command**: a redirect placement is built only when `proj.system.id !== LOCAL_SYSTEM_ID` (`src/instances.ts`), and `isRedirectable` duck-types on `execOneShot`, which `LocalSystem` does not implement — measured on both, and a bypass of either fails LOUD (`TypeError`), not silently. Consumers reach it either directly — `plugins/library.ts` hooks, `selfUpdate.ts` npm install, `gitLive.ts` (uncapped: git porcelain is parsed whole), all of which act on cc's OWN checkouts and are local by construction — or through `System.exec`, which is how `runGit`, the `worktrees.ts` post-worktree hook (keeping its own truncation-marker shaping and `timedOut`-flag result) and `project_bash` now run. Pinned by `tests/grouped-command.test.mjs`; `plugins/supervisor.ts` takes the group kill only, since it owns its long-lived child's lifecycle.

  **Two caps, opposite ends, because two callers need opposite things.** `cap` keeps the TAIL, per stream, in characters — a log's last N chars are what diagnoses a failure. `headCapBytes` keeps the HEAD, across both streams, in bytes, and keeps draining: `project_bash` truncates what is *shown* while letting the command run to completion, matching the built-in Bash tool. One caller wants one end: they are not enforced against each other, and setting both would simply compose two clips nobody has a use for.

  **`maxBufferBytes` is a third thing entirely — a fence, not a cap.** Past it the command is killed and the result is a FAILURE (`{code: 1}`) carrying exactly the first `maxBufferBytes` of output. It exists for callers that parse output WHOLE, where a clipped-but-successful result is read as the truth: worse than an error. The failure is set explicitly, not inferred from the kill — a command that finishes before the signal lands would otherwise report its own `0`. The diagnostic goes into `stderr` (appended if there is already one) because `stderr || stdout` is the near-universal caller shape here and an empty stderr would promote megabytes of partial output into an error message. It rides on `runGit` (see `GIT_OUTPUT_LIMIT_BYTES` below) and on `ProviderShell`'s non-persistent-shell fallback (the **Output fence** bullet above). `stdin: 'ignore'` hands the command a closed stdin so an interactive one sees EOF instead of hanging until the timeout (`project_bash`'s documented behaviour, and the same `< /dev/null` a redirected `Bash` will frame each command with — matching the CLI's own Bash tool, which takes no stdin parameter). **One deliberate non-adopter remains:** `restart.ts`'s `spawnReplacementAndExit` is not this idiom at all — a fire-and-forget `detached` + `unref()` replacement spawn with no capture, no timeout and no group kill.
- **src/jsonStore.ts** — `createJsonStore({file, noun, empty, parse, toDoc, isEmpty, unlinkWhenEmpty?, loadLenient?, loadStrict?, afterWrite?})` → `{load, mutate}`. The one read-modify-write machine behind every `<store>/*.json` session sidecar. `mutate(fn)` = `serialize( withLock( fn(loadStrict(), write) ) )`: the **lock** is the correctness guard (O_EXCL, `storeLock.ts` — it excludes same-process callers too), the **chain** is a contention guard keeping same-process writers off the lockfile so a burst can't exhaust the bounded acquire retries, and the strict re-read **inside** the lock is what makes the base canonical. `load()` is lenient (any failure → empty + warn, for the query surface); `loadStrict()` throws on anything but ENOENT so a transient read error can't be laundered into "the store was empty" and written back as truth. Writes go through `writeFileAtomic` (`src/projects.ts`, documented above) and unlink when empty by default. The returned surface is deliberately just those two: `loadStrict` and `write` stay INTERNAL, reachable only through `mutate`, because a public bare `write()` would be an UNLOCKED atomic write on the very primitive that exists to guarantee the lock. The three optional hooks exist for `archivedSessions.ts` alone (`.bak` recovery, always-write-a-document, rolling-backup refresh inside the lock) — see that module. **The in-mutation read is STRICT, and that is load-bearing:** with the LENIENT loader corrupt JSON degrades to an empty map that is then written back carrying only the new entry — silently discarding every other title/summary in the file. On `createJsonStore` every store reads via `loadStrict`, so corrupt JSON throws and the mutation aborts with the file untouched. This is the intended fail-loudly direction and matches what the four already-hardened stores did on main; a class of silent data loss became a loud failure. Pinned by `tests/store-lock-lost-update.test.mjs` (the cross-process lost update) and `tests/store-lock.test.mjs` (the lock primitive, including that the lock is held until `fn`'s BODY settles — `return await fn()`, not `return fn()`).
- **src/httpError.ts** — `httpError(statusCode, message, extra?)` → `Object.assign(new Error(message), {statusCode}, extra)`. The single constructor for the statusCode-bearing Error the express error handler (`routes.ts`) and `codeForStatus` (`mcp/server.ts`) read; `extra` carries the plugin routes' `{status, tail, retryAfter}` 503 fields. Every module that needs it imports from here — no local copies. (Node-style `{code:'ENOENT'}` errors are a different thing and stay hand-built.)
- **src/duration.ts** — `humanizeDuration(ms)` → compact `h`/`m`/`s`, largest-first, zero components dropped, truncated to whole seconds (`30m`, `1h30m`, `1m30s`, `<1s` sub-second, `0s` for 0/negative/non-finite), never `ms`. The renderer for conductor-facing timeout text: the heartbeat's "did NOT finish" stub (`src/idleSubscriptions.ts`) and the plugin MCP 504 (`src/plugins/mcpBridge.ts`). Distinct from `public/usage.js`'s `formatDuration` (spaced, two components max, `—` for null), which is UI-only and stays that way.
- **src/planApproval.ts** — `buildApprovePrompt(feedback)` / `buildRejectPrompt(feedback)`. Source-of-truth for the magic strings the UI's Approve & Implement / Reject buttons, the server-side auto-approve, and the MCP `approve_plan` / `reject_plan` tools all send to the worker.
- **public/userQuestionAnswers.js** — `formatUserQuestionAnswers(questions, answers)` (+ `parseUserQuestionAnswers` for replay). DOM-free source-of-truth for the AskUserQuestion answer text. Lives under `public/` (the only statically served dir) but is imported by **both** the browser (via `public/blocks.js`'s re-export → UI question card) and the server (`src/mcp/handlers.ts`'s `answer_question` tool), so an MCP answer is byte-identical to a UI submit — a *true* shared import, not a mirror. **Prefix-safety invariant:** answers are delivered unconditionally (mid-turn included), and `Instance.prompt`'s `MID_TURN_NOTE` rides as its OWN content block rather than a text prefix — `user_echo` is emitted from the raw text before the `unshift`, and every replay path drops the block via `isMidTurnNoteContent` (`src/parser.ts`) inside the shared `consolidateUserContent`. That is what keeps `isUserQuestionAnswerText`'s `startsWith` matching. Concatenating the note into the text instead would silently unpair every mid-turn answer from its card; pinned by `tests/mid-turn-card-answer.test.mjs`. The same invariant binds `POST_STOP_STEER_NOTE` (`src/instances.ts`), the note a steer delivered after a block-edge stop carries instead: it is built by splicing its extra clause **inside** `MID_TURN_NOTE`'s `<system-reminder>` wrapper, so `isMidTurnNoteContent`'s three shape signals hold by construction and the note is neither rendered as a second user bubble nor counted in the rewind/fork index; pinned by `tests/deferred-steer.test.mjs`.
- **public/wakeCallback.js** — the idle-wake wake-stub marker format (`WAKE_CALLBACK_MARKER`/`WAKE_BODY_SEP` + `buildWakeStub`/`parseWakeCallback`). Same cross-boundary shared-import pattern as `userQuestionAnswers.js`: `src/idleSubscriptions.ts` builds the folded stub, `public/conversation.js` parses it to render the collapsible wake-callback bubble. In-band marker (like `<transcribed>`/`SOFT_INTERRUPT_MARKER`) so it survives resume/replay.
- **src/plugins/** — the plugin subsystem ([features](features.md#plugins), [wire contracts](plugins.md)). **Trust stance: plugins are trusted own code** — same-origin iframes + supervised child processes with the conductor's privileges; there is no sandboxing. Modules:
  - **registry.ts** — `createPluginHost({instances})`, the single service layer shared by REST (`api.ts`), the reverse proxy and MCP forwarding. Owns discovery (iterate `listProjects()` — so an **adopted** out-of-root project carrying a manifest is discovered exactly like an in-root one — reading each project dir's manifest; a project whose main checkout has NO manifest file falls back to its worktree checkouts — first valid manifest in sorted-name order, via the store metadata with no git spawns — so a plugin-in-progress in an unmerged worktree can bootstrap (`manifestSource` marks the origin; enable then defaults `activeVersion` to that worktree; an invalid main manifest is never masked); duplicate id ⇒ first-alphabetical wins, rest `conflict`), lifecycle state (`stopped|starting|ready|crashed|failed` on top of `discovered/enabled⇄disabled` + `invalid|incompatible|conflict`), lazy `ensureStarted` (deduped in-flight start, exponential crash backoff and a crash-count threshold ⇒ `failed` (constants in `src/plugins/registry.ts`)), discovery-time CC-Dev workspace auto-assign (every rescan/boot places any unassigned discovered plugin project into CC-Dev, not just on enable — the conductor's own project gets the same placement via a separate one-time boot seed in `server.ts`, since it isn't a plugin), worktree-version activation (cwd resolved via `worktrees.ts` metadata; manifest re-read + id-checked from the active checkout every start), and **adopt-don't-drain**: children are detached process groups that survive the conductor's self-respawn — `init()` re-adopts a recorded pid that's alive+answering, else clears the record (the liveness predicate and the ready/adopted lifecycle mutation are here; the record pruning + its single write are the store's, see `store.ts` below). Health is passive (child exit events, proxy `reportUpstreamFailure`, on-demand `status()` probe) — no periodic poller (battery). Init is lazy + memoized **on success only** and keyed to `projectsRoot()` (test roots swap) — a rejected init clears the memo before rethrowing, so one transient failure (e.g. a boot-time EMFILE/EIO) doesn't poison every later plugin-host call for the process lifetime; the next call reinitializes instead. **Contributions-only plugins** (manifest with `conventions` but no backend) enable but never start — the row reports `hasBackend:false` and state `enabled` (not `stopped`), and start refuses 400. Convention/role/`claudePlugin` contributions are NOT here — they live in the composed collaborator `contributions.ts` below, which the `conventions()`/`roles()`/`claudePluginDirs()` members point straight at; the registry keeps no cache state and only signals change into it (`invalidate()` from `rescanInternal`/`doStart`/`setActiveVersion`/`enable`, `noteRegistryChange()` from `saveRegistry` and `doStart`'s manifest reassignment). It does own `contributingEntries()` — the enabled+`ok` filter, which reads both the discovery catalog and the persisted records — and injects it, along with `ensureInit` and **`resolvePlacement`** — where a contributing plugin's checkout is RIGHT NOW, resolved on every read rather than captured at rescan. Three answers: `{kind:'ok', system, dir, cwd}`; `{kind:'unregistered'}` when `resolveProjectDir` returns null AND `projectStoreDir(name)` is gone AND `orchStoreRoot()` is still there — and each of those two store probes is an `fs.stat` with a deliberate error direction, never `existsSync`, which answers false on every error and so cannot tell "gone" from "unreadable": for the project's store dir **only ENOENT is gone** (anything else must not read as authoritatively unregistered), while for the store ROOT any error reads as absent (which sends the classification to the degrade branch) (that order is fixed — `deleteProject` removes the store dir on all three of its branches and an `rm -rf` of a checkout removes neither, which is what makes the middle term the discriminator, and the last term is the guard that stops a vanished store root classifying **every** plugin as unregistered at once); else a THROW, which the caller degrades on. `resolveCwd` stays for `doStart`, and the pure `versionCwd(activeVersion, worktreeMeta, base)` line is shared by it, `describeRow` and `resolvePlacement`. `describeRow` is now a ~15-line gatherer over `row.ts` — note it re-resolves the active VERSION per call but not the PLACEMENT, so a row's `system`/`localOnly` badge stays as of the last rescan.
  - **contributions.ts** — `createContributions({ensureInit, contributingEntries, resolvePlacement})`, the composed collaborator owning everything enabled plugins contribute to the conductor beyond an HTTP backend: `conventions()`, `roles()`, `claudePluginDirs()`, BOTH caches (`fragmentBodyCache`, the memoized `conventions()` result) and the `registryGeneration` counter that invalidates the second — a counter whose only reader is that memo. The registry signals into it through `invalidate()` (drop bodies + bump) and `noteRegistryChange()` (bump only), and holds no cache of its own. `roles()` is deliberately **synchronous and init-free** (a role binding is inline in the manifest, and `appSettings.resolveRoleBackend` resolves it at spawn time; `server.ts` installs the provider before `init()`) — pinned by `tests/plugins-host-surface.test.mjs`. The `conventions()` accessor returns the contributions of enabled+`ok` plugins only (bodies read from the active checkout through the placement's own `System` handle, cached in a single process-lifetime `fragmentBodyCache` keyed `${system.id}\0${system.remoteId ?? ''}\0${abs}` — **taken off the handle the read goes through**, never from a placement stored elsewhere, because a key derived from a second source can be a correct key for a machine the read never spoke to, i.e. a stale body served as healthy. For the same reason the narrow `ContributingEntry` this module declares carries `{id, project, manifest}` and no `dir`/`system`/`remoteId` at all) — each entry carries `body` (fragment, `''` when the convention is scaffold-only) and optional `scaffold` (resolved directive text). Two distinct failure modes, treated differently: a **vanished fragment/scaffold file** is skipped with a warning, same as always — the convention just contributes nothing, same as if it were never declared. A **failed placement resolution** is transient infra (a worktree checkout not yet mounted, a system that is down, or a checkout that vanished while cc still holds store state for the project), not "contributes nothing", so it additionally flags `.degraded` on **every** scope array the function returns — including ones left empty — because an empty-and-unflagged array is exactly what "this scope genuinely has no plugin contributions" looks like, and the one plugin that failed may have been the only would-be contributor to that scope; see `fragmentCatalog.ts`'s `CatalogList` and the `src/projectClaudeMd.ts` bullet below for what a reader does with that. A third case sits between them: an **authoritatively unregistered** project (`resolvePlacement` → `{kind:'unregistered'}`) is skipped like a vanished file, NOT degraded — its absence is confirmed by cc's own local bookkeeping, and degrading would freeze every referencing `CONVENTIONS.md` until someone pressed Rescan, which in a running server is the only thing that retires a discovery entry. The cache is dropped wholesale (the registry calls `contributions.invalidate()`) at the start of `rescanInternal()`, at the top of `doStart()`'s async body, right after `setActiveVersion()`'s `saveRegistry()`, and right after `enable()`'s `saveRegistry()` — every explicit "the checkout on disk moved, or a disabled plugin's fragments are about to matter again" gesture (rescan, start/restart, an active-version switch even on a stopped/backendless plugin, and enable — a fragment can be edited while its plugin sits disabled) — so neither a `git pull` into an already-loaded checkout nor an edit made while disabled keeps serving stale fragment text for the process lifetime. `conventions()` itself is **memoized**, because it is not a cheap read (it walks `contributingEntries()` and calls `resolvePlacement()` per contributing plugin, which resolves the project and — for a worktree-pinned plugin — does a dynamic `import('../worktrees.ts')` plus a store read — and `server.ts` wires **two** providers onto it, so one `GET /api/settings/conventions/conductor` was two full scans and a mutating plugin route fanned out through `regenerateAllProjectConventions` at two scans per project). The invalidation signal has **three parts, all load-bearing**: (1) a `registryGeneration` counter, owned here and bumped through `noteRegistryChange()` from `saveRegistry()` (which every mutation of `persisted.plugins` is immediately followed by — so the set is provably complete rather than hand-enumerated, and it is what catches `disable()`, which deliberately does *not* drop fragment bodies), inside `invalidate()` (so a stale fragment body can never survive in a cached result), and — again via `noteRegistryChange()` — immediately after `doStart()`'s `entry.manifest = result.manifest` reassignment (which happens ten lines *after* that function's own invalidate, so a concurrent read landing in between would otherwise be cached pre-reassignment); and (2) a per-call `existsSync` liveness re-check of the checkout dirs the cached scan actually read — both the discovered project dir and the resolved cwd. A checkout deleted from under a running server mutates no registry state, so the counter alone cannot see it, and serving a cached "still here" would silently disable the `.degraded` flag that `ensureProjectConventionsMd`'s never-blanks decline depends on; a vanished dir simply falls through to the real scan, which degrades exactly as it does uncached; and (3) a per-call **placement fingerprint** — `systemHandleGeneration()` (`src/systems/registry.ts`, bumped whenever a live provider handle is dropped) joined with `placementToken(project)` (`src/projects.ts`) per contributing plugin. Part (2) exists because a project's TREE is state this host does not own; a project's PLACEMENT is the same kind of state, so this is that argument one step out rather than a third mechanism. `placementToken` is the cheap LOCAL prefix of `resolveProjectDir` — the record's `(system, remoteId, systemPath)` tuple for a non-local placement, else which local artefact registers the project (`root` / `ext:<realpath>` / `gone`) — and it reaches no system and never throws, because a version that resolved the system would throw on every compose while a box was down and flip the catalog permanently degraded. `systemHandleGeneration()` is the one term the per-plugin tokens cannot carry: re-pointing a system's provider command leaves the record, the fragment path and the cache KEY byte-identical while the machine behind them changes. **`fragmentBodyCache` is labelled PER ENTRY, and that is the whole of its correctness: a cached body is served only under the exact state it was read under.** Each entry is `{label, body}` where the label joins `cacheEpoch` (bumped by `invalidate()` — the bytes at a path may have changed under a placement that did not move: a `git pull`, a version switch, a fragment edited while disabled) with the placement fingerprint above (the path may now name a different tree — and `setProjectRemote`/`updateSystem` call no `invalidate()`, so neither half implies the other). The label is captured ONCE at the top of a scan and handed to every read it makes; `readFragment` is the one insert site and the one lookup site, and it always writes the label it was handed. A retired entry is kept out by **two deliberately redundant guards** — the lookup treats a non-matching label as a miss, and `conventions()` sweeps non-matching entries at the top of each scan. Neither is *the* guard: mutation proof measured that removing either one alone leaves the suite green, and only removing both reopens the hole, so they are kept together (opposite ends of the same property, for the price of a string compare). **A per-MAP label was tried and is not sufficient**: it is a claim made at one instant about a map written over a whole scan, and two interleaves broke it — a degraded compose discards the memoized result while leaving the bodies cached, and an `invalidate()` landing MID-SCAN voids the running scan's claim after some of its inserts have happened. Both ended as a body from one machine served for another at `degraded:false`. With the label on the entry, a scan whose label is voided while it runs cannot contribute a servable entry at all — its inserts are inert rather than mislabelled — so the property holds without any assumption about which other gesture fired when. `invalidate()` still clears the map outright, and bumps the epoch as well — clearing alone is undone by an in-flight scan repopulating the map, and the epoch is what makes those late inserts unservable. **A degraded result is not memoized at all** — degraded means "a transient failure stopped me telling you", and a cached transient needs an unrelated gesture to clear (a remote system coming back up changes neither the generation nor the fingerprint, so the catalog would keep declaring itself degraded, and keep every referencing `CONVENTIONS.md` frozen, until the next rescan); the re-scan cost is bounded by the fact that a degraded catalog is exactly the state in which the fan-out it feeds does not write. The generation is snapshotted **before** the scan loop, because `reconcileActiveVersion` can `saveRegistry()` mid-computation when it self-heals a vanished worktree pin — the result is then tagged stale and recomputed once, and the idempotent self-heal does not bump again. **The returned object is shared by reference and must be treated as read-only**; both consumers are verified non-mutating (`fragmentCatalog.ts` reads `.degraded` then copies every entry via `raw.map(r => ({...r, builtin:false}))`; `server.ts`'s two providers only index `.project` / `.conductor`). No defensive shallow copy is made — the entry objects would still be shared, so it would buy the appearance of safety rather than safety. `server.ts` wires it to the projectConventions and conductorConventions providers. `conventions()` is **grouped by the required per-entry `scope`** (keyed by `SUPPORTED_CONVENTION_SCOPES` from manifest.ts; `project`/`conductor` today, `workspace` rejected at load); `server.ts` routes the `project` group to `projectConventions` and the `conductor` group to `conductorConventions`, so adding a scope is a one-line enum move + one provider wiring, not a redesign.
  - **store.ts** — `createPluginStore({onRegistryChange})`, the persistence collaborator: the two JSON files under `<orchStoreRoot()>/plugins/` — `registry.json` `{plugins:{<id>:{project, enabled, activeVersion}}}` and `runtime.json` `{<id>:{pid, pgid, port, startedAt, gitHead}}` (both shapes pinned by `tests/plugins-store-files.test.mjs`) — plus their load policy and the adopt pass's record pruning. A non-ENOENT load failure of either is **not** swallowed: the bad file is renamed to `<file>.corrupt` — a fixed name, so a prior backup is overwritten — and recorded on `notices()`, which the registry re-exports, `GET /api/plugins` returns and the Settings → Plugins status line renders; without that, the `{plugins:{}}` fallback silently resets every plugin's enabled state and pinned version with nothing anywhere saying so. A failed rename is itself non-fatal — the notice carries `backup: null`. Notices reset per `load()` pass, and `load()` reads registry.json before runtime.json. **Reads hand out shallow copies and every mutator writes** (`upsert`/`setEnabled`/`setActiveVersion` for registry.json; `recordStart`/`clearRuntime`/`clearRuntimeDetached`/`adopt` for runtime.json) — that is what the boundary buys: no code outside this module can change persisted state without going through a method that persists it. `onRegistryChange` fires from the single registry.json save path, BEFORE the write (a failed write still leaves the contributions cache invalidated), which is what makes the invalidation set provably complete rather than hand-enumerated. `adopt({isAdoptable})` is **inverted** rather than lifted whole: the store iterates only its own records, asks the injected predicate, clears the rejects in one write (only when something changed) and returns the adopted ids for the registry to mark ready — moving the whole pass here would have needed back-references to discovery and lifecycle. The file paths are functions, recomputed per call, so a `PROJECTS_ROOT` swap between test roots is picked up.
  - **row.ts** — `buildPluginRow({entry, reg, runtime, record, activeVersion, cwd})` → `PluginRow`, the view-model `GET /api/plugins` serializes verbatim and `public/pluginManager.js` / `public/appSwitcher.js` read field by field (`crashTail`, `navLabel || name`, `frontendPath`, …). A **pure projection**: it writes nothing and closes over nothing, so every input is passed in and no shared mutable state crosses the boundary — which is what makes the 22-field contract explicit instead of implicit. The registry's `describeRow` is the gatherer: it resolves (and self-heals) the active version, computes the checkout `cwd`, reads the persisted record / runtime state / runtime record, and calls this. The `headSha` git spawn stays behind `state === 'ready' && record?.gitHead` — only a running plugin can have moved past the sha it started at; `cwd` itself is computed unconditionally by the caller (pure, no I/O). `describe`/`list`/`status`/`rescan` stay in the registry — they are catalog/lifecycle queries that happen to return rows. Row-level contributions metadata (`conventions:[{slug,name,description,hasScaffold}]`, namespaced) is built here for the Settings badges; `hasScaffold` flags a convention whose pick triggers a one-time setup directive in addition to any fragment. Field set pinned by `tests/plugins-row-shape.test.mjs`.
  - **manifest.ts** — `readManifest(dir)`/`validateManifest(json)` → normalized manifest or structured error list; rejects anything `validateArgs` can't validate (flat-object schema subset) at load time.
  - **supervisor.ts** — factory port of code-hub's runner: `bash -lc <start>` spawned `detached` (pgid = pid) with `$PORT`/`CONDUCTOR_URL`/`CONDUCTOR_PLUGIN_ID`/`PROJECTS_ROOT` (resolved) /`CONDUCTOR_PROJECT_DIR` (conductor's own checkout), readiness readyWhen-regex → healthPath → TCP within 30 s, 400 ms settle window + EADDRINUSE retry ×3 on fresh ports, a bounded output tail (`OUTPUT_CAP`, `src/plugins/supervisor.ts`), stop = `kill(-pgid, SIGTERM)` → SIGKILL after 3 s, `git rev-parse HEAD` recorded per start. **All three readiness modes share ONE `poll`** — the mode picks the predicate (regex / `httpOk` / `tcpOpen`), never the loop — and that loop is **cancelled when the child exits before readiness**, via an `abort` predicate that reads the same `status !== 'starting'` condition `settle()` guards on, so liveness has exactly one authority. Without that cancellation a child that dies *after* the settle window left the probe running for the full 30 s timeout, aiming HTTP GETs / connects at a port it never owned and that `allocatePort()` may already have reissued to another plugin (in tests it also leaked a ref'd `Socket`/`Timeout` past the file's own teardown). The bare-TCP mode's timeout error is now `readiness not confirmed within timeout` like the other two, not `waitForPort`'s `port … not listening within …`.
  - **ports.ts** — `allocatePort` (bind-:0-close), `tcpOpen` (one-shot connect probe → boolean; destroys the socket on both paths), `waitForPort` (`tcpOpen` on a poll loop — supervisor readiness no longer uses it; `registry.ts`'s `probeAnswers` does), `pidAlive`.
  - **proxy.ts** — hand-rolled streaming reverse proxy (no body parsing; SSE free) + raw-socket WebSocket upgrade pipe with `rawHeaders` order/casing replay; mounted after `/mcp`, before static.
  - **api.ts** — `/api/plugins` REST router, thin delegations to the registry and to `library.ts`, mounted from `routes.ts`. Also owns the project-conventions fan-out: every mutating route (`rescan`, `:id/enable`, `:id/disable`, `:id/restart`, `:id/version`, and the two library routes) calls a local `refreshProjectConventions()` after its underlying mutation succeeds, which best-effort-calls `regenerateAllProjectConventions({log:console})` (`projectClaudeMd.ts`) so every project's `CONVENTIONS.md` picks up the change — never throws (a regenerate failure must never turn a successful mutation into an error response); the `{log:console}` surfaces `ensureProjectConventionsMd`'s per-project log lines in the server log (contract + exact strings: the `src/projectClaudeMd.ts` bullet below).
  - **library.ts** — `createPluginLibrary({pluginHost, _cloneImpl, _runHookImpl})`, the Plugin Library collaborator: a catalog of installable plugins (git repo URLs) separate from the discovery-based registry above. `list()` merges built-in `code-share`/`code-playwright`/`code-hub`/`code-karpathy-wiki`/`code-kanban`/`code-dialectic`/`code-mutant`/`code-system` defaults with drop-in manifests from `<orchStoreRoot()>/plugins/library/*.json` (external entries override a built-in with the same `id`; malformed/incomplete files are skipped with a warning), each row flagged `installed`/`installedAs` by checking whether its derived target directory already exists under `projectsRoot()`. `install()`'s own "already installed" probe goes through `resolveProjectDir` rather than an in-root `fs.stat`, so an **adopted** project holding that name refuses the install too instead of minting a second identity for one name. `install(id)` validates the repo URL scheme (`http:`/`https:`/`git:` only), derives the target project name from the URL's last path segment (`.git` stripped, run through `projects.ts`'s `validateName`), refuses if the target already exists (409), clones via the shared streaming `runGitLive` (`src/gitLive.ts`; bounded timeout, never throws — `{code,stdout,stderr}` shape), cleans up a partial directory on failure (502 + stderr tail) so a retry isn't permanently blocked, and on success calls `pluginHost.rescan()`, **enables the freshly-discovered plugin by default** (only a `state:'discovered'` row — an invalid/conflicting manifest is left disabled; `enable()` is start-neutral so no process launches at install time), then runs the entry's `postClone` if set. `update(id)` is the symmetric counterpart: 404 if not installed, `git pull --ff-only` via the same `runGitLive`, 502+tail on pull failure (never attempts a merge), else rescan (drops the registry's fragment-body cache), run `postPull`, then resolve the eligible-to-restart set via `resolveRunningIds(pluginHost, projectName)` (reads `pluginHost.list()`, filters to this project's rows whose `state` is `ready`/`starting`) **before** looking at `postPull`'s outcome — this ordering matters: an empty set always reports `null` regardless of `postPull`, so "nothing was running" is never mislabeled as a skip. A non-empty set with a failed `postPull` skips the restart outright — `restarted = {skipped:'postPull-failed'}` — rather than attempting it, because a failed `postPull` leaves the checkout half-built and restarting into it would trade a healthy child serving old code for a crash-looping one serving no code at all; otherwise `restartIds(pluginHost, ids, onChunk)` restarts (`pluginHost.restart(id)`) each of them and returns `{ids, ok, error}` on success/failure; never throws (a restart failure is soft, exactly like a failed `postPull` — the pull already landed on disk) and never starts a stopped plugin (it'll pick up the new code lazily on its next start). `update()`'s result gains a `restarted: {ids,ok,error} | {skipped:'postPull-failed'} | null` field carrying that value. Update **detection** in `list()` runs a bounded best-effort `fetchOriginBounded` (`src/gitLive.ts`) then `getProjectUpstreamStatus` (`worktrees.ts`) per installed row, flagging `updateAvailable`/`behind`. Both `postClone`/`postPull` go through a shared `runHook()`/`runHookCommand()` pair: `runHookCommand` runs the command via `spawn('bash', ['-lc', cmd], {detached:true})` with a process-group kill on timeout (mirrors `worktrees.ts`'s `runPostWorktreeHook`, not `cloneRepo`'s simpler `execFile` timeout — a hook like `npm install` can spawn grandchildren a plain child-kill would orphan) and never rejects; `runHook()` wraps it into `{ran, ok, code, tail}` (or `null` if the entry has no such field). Failure handling is deliberately asymmetric: a failed clone/pull is a hard failure (reject + rollback for clone); a failed post-hook or restart is a **soft warning** on an otherwise-successful `install()`/`update()` response — the clone/pull already succeeded and is already discoverable, so the result resolves with `postClone`/`postPull: {ok:false, tail}` / `restarted: {ok:false, error}` instead of throwing.
  - **mcpBridge.ts** — composed collaborator turning enabled plugins' manifest tools into core-shaped entries (`<plugin-id>__<tool>`); `buildMcpRouter` composes `ctx.tools = [...coreTools, ...pluginHost.toolsFor()]` per request, so dynamism (enable/disable) lands without touching tools/list / tools/call. Every enabled plugin's tools are **globally visible** — offered to the conductor UI and to workers in every project, so `toolsFor()` takes no caller argument; the manifest `mcp.scope` field is accepted for compatibility with shipped manifests but dropped at load. The caller's identity/project still rides each forwarded call's `caller` payload.
  - Frontend counterparts: `public/appSwitcher.js`, `public/pluginView.js` (built on `hashView.js`'s additive `matchHash` predicate), `public/pluginBridge.js` (classic script served at root, included by plugins), `public/pluginManager.js` (settings group, installed by `settings.js`).
- **src/routes.ts** — Thin REST shell; hosts hook-callback, attachment streaming, `/admin/restart`, worktree sync/merge.
- **src/restart.ts** — `scheduleRestart()`: close WSS + http, temp-wipe (delegated to `runTempCleanup()`), spawn detached child with same argv/env/cwd, exit. Child's listen-with-retry handles `EADDRINUSE`. `spawnReplacementAndExit()` is the shared detached-spawn-then-exit tail (also used by the resume path). `runTempCleanup({instances, log})` (exported separately so it's unit-testable without triggering `process.exit`) handles temp cleanup in two parts: (1) live-attached temps via `InstanceManager.shutdownTempSync()`, which SIGKILLs every live temp claude (SIGTERM would let it flush a final line that re-creates the `O_APPEND|O_CREAT` jsonl), reaps each pid, and deletes the jsonl's `subagents/` dir; (2) crash-orphaned temps — sessionIds recorded in `temp-sessions.json` (`tempSessions.ts`) with no live instance, found via `orphanedTempIdsSync()` diffed against the live snapshot — archived directly (`markArchived`/`unmarkTemp`, no cwd on record so no dir to clean). Both groups are folded into one `<store>/pending-temp-cleanup.json` manifest write (cwd-less for orphaned entries) so the next boot's `sweepPendingTempCleanup` can retry anything the fire-and-forget sidecar writes didn't land in time for (manifest unlinked per successful sweep). "Restart + Resume" (`resumeRestart.ts`) never calls `runTempCleanup` — temps are carried over there instead.
- **src/gitLive.ts** — shared git-subprocess helpers for the paths that pull/fetch a managed checkout: `runGitLive(args, cwd, {timeoutMs, onChunk})` (streamed spawn, detached process-group timeout/kill, resolves `{code,stdout,stderr}`, never rejects), `fetchOriginBounded(cwd)` (bounded best-effort `git fetch` for update-detection, `NO_PROMPT_GIT_ENV` so private-repo auth fails fast). Imported by both `plugins/library.ts` and `selfUpdate.ts` — one implementation, no per-surface drift.
- **src/selfUpdate.ts** — conductor self-update service (Settings → About), the app's own version of the Plugin Library update path. `getSelfUpdateStatus({repoRoot})` → `{version, upstream, behind, canCheck, updateAvailable}` (version from `package.json`; bounded `fetchOriginBounded` then `getProjectUpstreamStatus` on the repo root — `canCheck:false` when detached/no-upstream). `applySelfUpdate({repoRoot, npmCmd, onChunk, onValidated})` runs `git pull --ff-only` (streamed `runGitLive`), diffs the moved files, runs `npm install` iff `package.json`/`package-lock.json` changed, returns `{ok, version, depsChanged, npm, restartRequired:true}`. **It never restarts** — the client (`settings.js` → `restartFlow.js` `performRestart({resume:true})`) hands off to the existing restart+resume engine after a successful apply, so a self-update reuses `restart.ts`/`resumeRestart.ts` wholesale rather than reimplementing drain/respawn/health-poll. Repo root resolves from `import.meta.url` (one level up from `src/`), overridable per-call and via `SELF_UPDATE_REPO_ROOT` (test seam); `npmCmd` defaults to `SELF_UPDATE_NPM_CMD || 'npm install'`.
- **src/resumeManifest.ts** — read/write/clear the **Resume after restart** manifest at `<store>/pending-resume.json` (mirrors `tempCleanup.ts`). `writeResumeManifest(entries)` (sync, mkdir's the store), `readResumeManifest()` → `{instances:[]}` (empty on absent/corrupt; removes a corrupt file), `clearResumeManifest()`. Each entry carries the spawn descriptor (`project/sessionId/cwd/mode/effort/thinking/model/worktreeName/temp/conducted/debug/title/firstPrompt/autoApprovePlan/group/wasBusy`, `workers` for conductors) plus the **pending overage auto-resume** state (`overageResumeAt` = fire deadline epoch secs, `overageStopped` = stop-resume intent, `overageResetsAt` = window reset epoch secs; all falsy when no resume is pending, `overageQueue` = messages queued during the wait window) — see protocol.md.
- **src/resumeRestart.ts** — the graceful resume path. `drainAndScheduleRestart()` (old process): **soft-interrupt** every mid-turn instance (`Instance.interrupt()` — the armed SOFT tier, landing at the model's next block edge), then `waitAllIdle` with `RESUME_DRAIN_GRACE_MS`=60 s: on timeout it logs a warning, **escalates** every live instance not already `turnForceAborted` to `interrupt({force:true})`, and re-arms *the grace window*. No status re-check here — both tiers are contracted to no-op off-turn (protocol.md → Two-tier interrupt), pinned for the forced tier by `tests/instances.test.mjs` → *forced interrupt is a no-op when not in a turn*. The soft arms themselves are bounded too — the drain passes `{ deadlineMs: softInterruptDeadlineMs() }`, so an arm that can never reach a block boundary (a wedged tool; a gateway framing the whole turn as one never-closed block) escalates on its own deadline instead of holding the restart open forever. A stop, not a wind-down steer — a steer is silently dropped by a model declaring `midTurnSteering:false`, and with an unbounded wait that hung the restart forever; the trade is that the human loses the in-transcript `user_echo` explaining the stop, and the boot-side `RESUME_TEXT` / `buildConductorResumeText` re-prompt is its sole owner now. **Then** tear down networking, `writeResumeManifest` (NO temp wipe), `shutdownForResumeSync`, `spawnReplacementAndExit`. Split out as `drainToManifest()` (steps 1–6, no exit) for testability. `restoreFromResumeManifest()` (new process, on boot): read+unlink manifest, three-group split (conductor / conducted worker / other), staggered `--resume` respawns (`RESUME_STAGGER_MS`=1.5 s), per-instance resume-notification prompt (conductor variant embeds the injected worker list via `buildConductorResumeText`). Owns the resume message texts (`RESUME_TEXT` / `buildConductorResumeText`); the wind-down texts are gone with `windDown`. **Overage auto-resume survival**: a session with a pending overage resume re-arms the wall-clock sweep on boot — *after* it's live + idle (so `spawn()`'s flag-clear can't wipe it) via `instances._armRestoredAutoResume(inst, overageResumeAt*1000)` (fire-and-forget, like the resume notification; `OverageResumeController.armRestored` bypasses `arm()`'s past-deadline skip). Queued messages are restored onto `inst._overageQueue` **before** the re-arm so `armRestored`'s status emit carries the restored `queuedCount`, and they flush with the resume. A deadline that elapsed while the orchestrator was down fires on the first post-boot sweep tick (`AUTO_RESUME_TEXT`); the `RESUME_TEXT` re-prompt is skipped for these sessions to avoid a double-prompt. **Limitation (out of scope):** this only covers the graceful "Resume after restart" path — a hard crash / external kill / `EADDRINUSE`-failed restart writes no manifest, so neither the session nor its overage resume is restored (the session must be manually resumed). Crash-survival would need a durable per-session overage sidecar (cf. `markTemp`/`isTemp`) re-armed on manual resume.
- **src/waitFor.ts** — `waitFor({initial, subscribe, timeoutMs, onTimeout})`: the one promise shape behind every LISTENER-driven wait. `initial()` is the already-satisfied fast path, checked before any listener is attached (returns `{value}` or `null`); `subscribe(settle, fail)` attaches whatever listeners it likes and returns an opaque teardown that detaches all of them — which is why the N-listener case needs no special support; `onTimeout()` picks the timeout's disposition by RETURN TYPE — an `Error` rejects, anything else resolves. The point is that the `clearTimeout` + teardown pair is written **once** and runs exactly once, on whichever of settle/fail/timeout fires first, instead of being hand-rolled on both paths at each call site. Adopter — the module's sole importer now: `waitForIdleOnce` + `waitAllIdle` (`resumeRestart.ts` — the per-instance and all-idle drains, both **resolving** on timeout, with `'timeout'` / `{timedOut:true, stragglers}` respectively). Neither passes `fail`, and neither returns an `Error` from `onTimeout` (`resumeRestart.ts` — both resolve), so `waitFor`'s `fail` callback and its reject-on-timeout disposition have **no production caller** — the Error/reject path is currently exercised only by `tests/wait-for.test.mjs`. Deliberately **not** adopted by the two POLLING loops (`plugins/registry.ts` `waitSettled`, `plugins/supervisor.ts` `poll`): both re-enter a `setTimeout` over a synchronous accessor or an HTTP/TCP/regex probe, so there is no emitter and nothing to detach — the supervisor's `abort` is likewise a predicate polled at each tick, not a listener — the helper's entire value is nil for them, and unifying those two with each other would need a three-knob helper for two call sites.
