- **src/worktrees.ts** — `createWorktree(project, {baseWorktree?, name?})` captures `{baseBranch, baseSha, branch}` from the **base's** HEAD — the project root, or another worktree of the project when `baseWorktree` names one (then `parentPath` is that worktree's checkout and `baseWorktree` is recorded; chains nest to **any depth** — `baseWorktree` has exactly one write site, this meta literal, written at creation and naming a record that already exists, so creation order is a topological order, the base graph is a DAG by construction, and no cycle check exists or is needed; a re-parenting API would end that) — resolving that base through the store records (`listWorktrees`), never by assembling a path, since `getProject` validates only the name charset and directory existence. `name` is slugified by `slugifyWorktreeName` into the branch + dir name in place of `shortId()`, with a collision on the branch ref `refs/heads/code-conductor/<slug>` refused 409 — one check covering both collision cases, since dir name and branch derive from the same slug and a registered worktree always still holds its branch; it buys a clean 409 rather than `git worktree add`'s 500, not cleanup. Then writes `worktree.json` into the central store and `git worktree add` off the captured SHA; after the worktree is fully set up it runs `runPostWorktreeHook(meta)` — the `post-worktree-create.sh` hook resolved in priority order from the **in-tree parent checkout** (`<meta.parentPath>/.code-conductor/…`, need not be committed) else the **central store** (`projectStoreDir(meta.parentProject)/post-worktree-create.sh`), run with `cwd` in the new worktree (non-fatal, always resolves, result attached to return as `postWorktreeCreate` with `source: 'in-tree' | 'store'`). `syncWorktree` picks no-op / FF / `git rebase --rebase-merges` / `commit-required` / `rebase-conflict`, and prompts nobody — the two blocked results carry `buildRebasePrompt(meta, blocker)`'s rendered text for the caller to dispatch (the flag is in both of its briefs; all three sites must carry it or the conflict path flattens the merge commits the automated path preserved). **`listWorktrees` is STORE-DERIVED**, enumerating `<store>/projects/<name>/worktrees/` (`worktreesStoreRoot`), with git used only as a FILTER to prune a worktree removed out-of-band. A registration is cc's own record, not a git fact, so it must list whether or not the system answers — gating the whole enumeration on `isGitRepo` made a degraded row show NO worktrees, which then told the user at the delete confirmation that it would unregister zero of them while one was registered. When git cannot answer (a system refusal, and only that — any other fault still throws) no filter is applied. **ONE SPELLING PER WORKTREE**: the store key, the `worktreeName` and the directory basename are one string — `slugifyWorktreeName(name)` or `shortId()` — so there is no alias to resolve and no name to parse out of a path. `listWorktrees`' `path.basename` live-prune is correct *because* of that identity, and `worktreePathFor` is the one place a checkout's location is decided: `<projectsRoot>/.worktrees/<project>/<key>` for a local project wherever its tree lives (cc owns that area; the user's parent directory is not cc's to scatter checkouts in), and for one on a system that system's `worktreesDir` else `<dirname(project path)>/.worktrees/<project>/<key>` — same shape, only the root differs, and no per-kind exception. Everything downstream of a resolution passes `meta.worktreeName`: `createWorktree` persists `base.worktreeName` as `baseWorktree` (the foreign key `listDependentWorktrees` matches on), `removeWorktree` `fs.rm`s `worktreeStoreDir(project, meta.worktreeName)`, and the two delete entry points (`routes.ts` DELETE, `mcp/handlers.ts` `deleteWorktree`) resolve once at the top before `instances.idsForWorktree` — an exact in-memory compare that would otherwise report no attached instances and skip the live-instance guard. `listDependentWorktrees` walks the **whole subtree, deepest-first** (downward-only over one `listWorktrees` load, `seen`-guarded like `agentTreeBackends`; level order, never `createdAt`) — transitive for the MESSAGE, not for the GATE: a subtree is empty exactly when the direct-child set is, so no operation is newly permitted or refused, and what the walk buys is a refusal that names the real blocker on the first call instead of a child whose own delete then refuses. It + `dependentsRefusal` back the `WORKTREE_HAS_DEPENDENTS` soft refusal minted once here and passed through un-reworded by both surfaces (unlike `WORKTREE_BEHIND`, which each surface re-words); it gates `syncWorktree` (before any status computation), `mergeWorktreeIntoParent` (gate 0, ahead of `WORKTREE_BEHIND`), and `removeWorktree` (ahead of the dirty check, so a refused delete has touched neither dir, branch, nor store entry). The delete gate is split across two layers, exactly like the dirty check: `removeWorktree` **throws** 409 with the same `reason` — which is what covers the REST/GUI delete path (`DELETE /api/projects/:name/worktrees/:wt`), the surface that would otherwise orphan children — while the MCP `deleteWorktree` handler pre-checks the same predicate to keep the soft `{ok:false, code, dependents}` channel. `force` skips both (it is the whole reason `removeAllWorktreesForProject`'s `{force:true}` cascade can still sweep a feature/task graph). **`removeWorktree`'s step order is load-bearing**: `git worktree remove --force`, then `dropWorktreeStoreEntry`, then the best-effort `git branch -d/-D`. The store entry is dropped BEFORE the branch delete because `runGit` throws a system refusal when git could not be run or never answered (`local` included) — written last, that throw left cc's registration behind for a worktree git had already forgotten, and `listWorktrees` (which prunes by `git worktree list`) then disagreed with `registeredWorktreeNames` (which does not consult git), so `setProjectRemote` refused 409 `PROJECT_PLACEMENT_IN_USE` naming a worktree the listing did not show (measured), and the retry could not clear it either, since `getWorktree` prunes the same way and 404s. The branch delete's refusal is re-raised ANNOTATED, not swallowed: `statusCode` / `code` / `systemRefusal` preserved, message asserting only what the code measured (`worktree '…' was removed, but its branch '…' may still exist`) — the branch clause is hedged because `GIT_TIMED_OUT` means the answer never arrived, so the far side may have deleted it — swallowing it would report a transport failure as a clean removal. Git's own non-zero exit there stays unread, which is what best-effort means here. The guarantee covers the GIT steps; its bound is stated once, at the `dropWorktreeStoreEntry` call site in `src/worktrees.ts`. **Every guard here reads a FAILED check as UNKNOWN, never as passed** — `removeWorktree`'s dirty gate, the MCP `deleteWorktree` precheck (`WORKTREE_DIRTY_UNKNOWN`), merge's parent-status gate (`PARENT_STATUS_UNKNOWN`) and merge's step-4 gate on the worktree's own tree (`WORKTREE_STATUS_UNKNOWN`) all refuse rather than proceed, because `if (dirty.ok && lines.length)` let a check that never ran authorise `git worktree remove --force`, and let a merge silently not land the uncommitted work step 4 exists to protect. The same rule holds on the READ surfaces — for a command that RAN and answered non-zero, which is the only way these degraded shapes are reached, since one that never ran or never answered throws a system refusal instead (`GIT_DID_NOT_RUN` 502 / `GIT_TIMED_OUT` 504): `project_status` reports `dirtyUnknown` rather than an empty `dirty` list; `getProjectCommits` reports `hasUncommitted: undefined` + `uncommittedUnknown` rather than `false`, and **throws** on a `git log` that did not answer — distinguished from a legitimately empty history by asking `hasUnbornHead` ONLY on that failure path, so the normal case pays nothing; `project_diff`'s summary reports `uncommitted: {unknown, reason}` rather than zeroed totals, matching what its own committed half already did by throwing; and a listing row runs both its git probes inside one try so a death BETWEEN them sets `systemUnreachable` instead of inventing `unbornHead: false`. `force:true` is still the override of a KNOWN-dirty tree. **`git merge` is NOT individually atomic**, contrary to the original design note: killed mid-merge, the orphaned command was observed completing the merge commit AFTER cc had reported failure — parent HEAD moved, `MERGE_HEAD` left set, worktree branch never fast-forwarded. So `mergeWorktreeIntoParent` wraps its WHOLE body (not just the prelude) and flags `mayHaveCompleted` on any refusal raised after the merge was issued, and the state that leaves behind is reported as `PARENT_MID_MERGE` — `PARENT_DIRTY`'s "commit or stash" is the wrong repair for a half-finished merge. `mergeWorktreeIntoParent` runs `git merge --no-ff --no-edit` with safety checks, then best-effort fast-forwards the worktree's own branch (`git merge --ff-only`, run from `meta.worktreePath` since that's where the branch is checked out) up to the new merge commit — always a valid FF since the worktree branch is one of the merge commit's parents — reporting the outcome as `worktreeFastForwarded` without affecting the overall `ok` result. Also `getProjectCommits` (`git log` on HEAD, bounded by `COMMITS_DEFAULT_LIMIT`/`COMMITS_MAX_LIMIT`, `src/worktrees.ts`) — which reads the project's tree, or the worktree its optional `worktree` option names, resolving both through `resolveProjectCwd` and taking the `aheadOf` base-branch fallback from the `worktreeMeta` that resolution returns, so nothing has to reverse-scan the store for a record matching a path — the diff surface itself moved to `src/gitDiff.ts` (below). Hook env vars: `CC_WORKTREE_PATH`, `CC_PROJECT_NAME`, `CC_BRANCH`, `CC_BASE_BRANCH`, `CC_PARENT_PATH`. Kill-switch: `ORCH_DISABLE_POST_WORKTREE_HOOK=1`. Timeout: `ORCH_POST_WORKTREE_TIMEOUT_MS` (default in `src/worktrees.ts`). Output capped to a tail bound (`HOOK_OUTPUT_CAP`, `src/worktrees.ts`). The hook runs BEFORE `Instance.spawn()` so it cannot interfere with the 5 s control-request timeout.

  **`runGit(system, cwd, args)` is fenced at `GIT_OUTPUT_LIMIT_BYTES` (16 MB), and it fails past it rather than truncating.** Every caller parses git's output whole — porcelain, numstat, a unified diff — so a clipped parse would be handed back as the truth; and cc is a single process hosting every worker session, where an unbounded retention is a real memory failure (measured: 37 MB of output held a 37 MB string at 248 MB RSS, and a ~150 MB diff extrapolates to ~1 GB). Crossing the fence surfaces through the paths callers already have: a 500 from the diff surface, an empty history from `getProjectCommits`. Pinned by `tests/worktrees.test.mjs` → "runGit fences git output".
- **src/gitDiff.ts** — owns the REST diff surface for all three git-history views (worktree review, single-commit, uncommitted working tree): `DIFF_BYTE_CAP` (per-file raw-diff cap, 200 KB) and `FILE_DIFF_LINE_GUARD` (20,000 changed-line ceiling checked from `--numstat` before the unified diff is ever read, so a pathological file can't reach `runGit`'s 16 MB `maxBuffer`), `assertValidBaseRef` (moved here from `worktrees.ts`), `parseUnifiedDiff` (raw `git diff`/`show` → per-file structured hunks), `parseNumstat`/`parseNameStatus` (also moved here, from `mcp/diffPaging.ts` — shared with `project_diff`'s summary mode). A `DiffTarget` seam (`{cwd, argv(opts, pathspec)}`) abstracts the three surfaces' git invocations (worktree `diff <ref>...HEAD`, commit `show [--first-parent] <sha>`, uncommitted `diff HEAD`) behind one `summarizeTarget()` (parallel `--numstat`/`--name-status`, zipped by index, never capped) and one `fileDiffForTarget()` (numstat → binary/oversized short-circuit → unified diff → byte-cap-truncate at a line boundary → `parseUnifiedDiff`, keeping path/status/adds/dels from the numstat+name-status pass so the two calls can't disagree). Exports the three summary builders (`getWorktreeDiff`/`getCommitDiff`/`getProjectUncommittedDiff`) and their per-file counterparts (`getWorktreeFileDiff`/`getCommitFileDiff`/`getProjectUncommittedFileDiff`), consumed by `routes.ts`'s three `?path=`-dispatching diff routes. Imports `runGit`/`getWorktree` from `worktrees.ts` and `getProject` from `projects.ts` — one-way (`gitDiff.ts → worktrees.ts`, never the reverse) so there's no import cycle.
- **src/attachments.ts** — `saveAttachment(project, worktreeName, {name, dataBase64})` → central-store path + abs `promptPath`. `isImageType()` classifies for image vs path-reference text blocks.
- **src/transcribe.ts** — `isAvailable()` (both binaries on disk?) and `transcribe(audioBuf)`. Spawns `ffmpeg -ac 1 -ar 16000 -f wav` → `whisper-cli -m <model> -f <wav> -of <prefix> --output-txt --no-prints`, reads the `.txt` sidecar, removes the tmpdir. Active-model resolution: `WHISPER_MODEL` env (explicit path) → the model chosen in Settings (`settings.json`) → built-in default. Install root from `INSTALL_ROOT` (defaults to the central store `<store>`), mirroring `bin/install-whisper.sh`; binary from `WHISPER_CLI`, ffmpeg from `FFMPEG_BIN`. Build internals: `bin/install-whisper.sh` `pkg install`s ffmpeg + build tools and clones+builds whisper.cpp under `<store>/whisper.cpp/`, downloading the `WHISPER_MODEL_NAME` model (default `ggml-small.en-q5_1.bin`).
- **src/appSettings.ts** (policy: [docs/models.md](models.md)) — read/write `<store>/settings.json` (cached, atomic writes). `getTranscribeModel()` / `setTranscribeModel(name)`, `getTierBackend(tier)` / `setTierBackend(tier, {backend,model})` (tier→backend binding), `getEnabledTiers()` / `setTierEnabled(tier, enabled)`, `getDefaultSpawnTier()` / `setDefaultSpawnTier(tier)`, `defaultSpawnBinding()` (= `getTierBackend(getDefaultSpawnTier())`, the binding a model-less fresh spawn runs on when it names no row at all — shared by `resolveSpawnModel` and the `POST /api/instances` route, and reused internally by `resolveRoleBackend`'s dead-Claude revert); namespaced (`transcribe`, `models`) for future groups. **The backend registry** lives in `models.backends: [{id,label,template,env}]`: `getBackends()` (managed rows are fully code-authoritative — id/label/template/env all from code; nothing on a managed row is stored) / `getBackend(id)` / `getSubstitutionBackends()` / `addBackend` / `updateBackend` / `removeBackend` (**never cascades** — throws 409 while a custom model still references it, and a second 409 while any *tracked* instance is still on it, via the `setLiveBackendsProvider(() => instances.liveBackendUsage())` seam wired in `server.ts` so this store never imports the instance registry). **Custom models** live in `models.customModels: [{label,model,backend,contextWindow}]` (the model id is the identity AND the exact lookup key — never normalized; `contextWindow` is required): `getCustomModels()` / `addCustomModel(…)` / `removeCustomModel(model)` / `isKnownBackendModel(backend, model)` / `contextWindowForModel(model)` (exact-id match; wrapped by `resolveContextWindowTokens({backend, model})`, the single place a session's capacity is resolved) / `backendForModel(model)`. A tier binding is `{backend, model}`; `getTierBackend` returns a **valid** binding verbatim (no silent revert) and falls back to the tier's default Claude binding only for an invalid/dead one (unknown Claude version, or a removed model/backend). There is **no** per-family active-version setting anymore — a tier stores a concrete version id directly. **Roles** are a parallel bindable layer: `getRoleBinding`/`setRoleBinding`/`resolveRoleBackend` back built-in + user-custom roles (binding in `models.roleBackend`), with **name-only** custom roles in `models.customRoles:[name]` (a string list — no label; the name is the display) via `getCustomRoles`/`addCustomRole`/`removeCustomRole`. A custom role starts on the `powerful` tier and is rebindable via `setRoleBinding`. **Role NAME matching is case-insensitive** (a spawn caller may type `MyRole` for a role stored `myrole`): names are stored/displayed as typed but matched, deduped, and collision-checked by lowercase (`canonicalBuiltinRole`/`canonicalCustomRole` return the stored key for a case-insensitive lookup). The custom-role name rule (`^[A-Za-z][A-Za-z0-9-]*$`) is deliberately distinct from the lowercase plugin-slug rule (`manifest.ts`) — a custom role name is a case-preserving user label, a plugin slug is a lowercase identifier. **Plugin-owned roles are live-derived, never persisted**: a provider injected by `server.ts` (`setPluginRolesProvider(() => pluginHost.roles())`, `getPluginRoles()`) keeps this store free of a plugin-registry import, so disabling a plugin drops its roles automatically. `getAllRoles()` merges the three (built-in/plugin carry `label`; custom entries are `{role}` name-only); `isResolvableRole()` is the dynamic case-insensitive guard `spawnInstance` uses in place of the static `isKnownRole`; `resolveRoleBackend` resolves a plugin role from its inline manifest binding (re-guarding a retired Claude model → default spawn tier) and a custom role's dead binding to the default spawn tier. **Default effort** is the second axis on those same rows (`models.tierEffort`, `models.roleEffort`; policy + precedence: [docs/models.md](models.md#default-effort)): `getTierEffort`/`setTierEffort`, `getRoleEffort`/`setRoleEffort` (role names canonicalized case-insensitively like the bindings), `inheritedRoleEffort` (what a role's `'inherit'` resolves to — exported only so `modelsSettingsState` can ship it as the UI's `Inherit (<level>)` label), `resolveRoleEffort`, and **`resolveSpawnEffort({effort, tier, role})` — the single resolution point for spawn effort, called by `InstanceManager._doCreate` and nothing else** (it owns the `400 invalid effort` validation too, so an invalid explicit value can't decay into a default). **The system registry** is its own namespace, `systems.registry: [{id,label,launch?}]`, on the same managed-row contract as the backend registry: `getSystems()` / `getSystem(id)` / `isKnownSystem(id)` / `addSystem` / `updateSystem` / `removeSystem`, with `local` fully code-authoritative (from `MANAGED_SYSTEMS` in `src/systems/registry.ts`, never persisted, not editable, not removable) and ids validated by `isSlug`. `getSystems` dedupes stored rows by id (**first wins**, matching the writers, which rewrite the list filtered by id) — only reachable by hand-editing, since `addSystem` 409s a duplicate. `removeSystem` **never cascades** — it throws 409 NAMING the projects whose record still points at the system (via `projectsBySystem()`), because a dangling `system` field resolves to nothing and cannot be repaired without knowing which projects were affected. `launch` is the provider command as argv, and it is **verified before it is persisted** (`verifySystemLaunch` → `probeSystemLaunch`): cc spawns the command, completes the handshake and throws the connection away, so an unreachable system is a 502 quoting the provider rather than a saved row that fails on the first project put on it. Re-verified only when the command CHANGES — a relabel must not fail because the system is currently down — and a change also disposes the live handle. A row with no `launch` is registration only: it declares that an execution environment exists and what to call it, and a project on it refuses `SYSTEM_NO_PROVIDER` at resolution.
- **src/projectSuggestions.ts** — the scan behind `GET /api/projects/suggestions` and the Adopt dialog's list: which directories under the projects root are **not** registered. A separate module from `src/projects.ts` because filesystem discovery of unregistered directories is a second responsibility, and the edge runs one way — it imports the registry (`projectsRoot`, `listProjects({includeConduct:true})`, `LOCAL_SYSTEM_ID`), never the reverse. `suggestAdoptableDirs({maxDepth?, maxDirs?})` → `{root, maxDepth, truncated, unreadable, candidates[]}`; both options exist only as a TEST SEAM (the cap test would otherwise need 2000 directories) and the route passes neither. `suggestedNameFor(basename)` encodes the two rules `registerProject` enforces (`NAME_RE`'s charset, and the dot-leading refusal) so the endpoint cannot suggest a name the server would refuse; `null` when nothing usable is left. The rules, the caps and the wire shape are in [protocol.md](protocol.md) → `GET /api/projects/suggestions`. **Its correctness condition is agreement with `adoptProject`** — a candidate the backend would refuse is a broken suggestion — so every rule is derived from one of that function's refusals and `tests/project-suggestions.test.mjs` adopts every candidate a fixture root yields. **`node:fs`, not a `System` handle, and that is not an exception to Conventions rule 8**: the seam is for PROJECT-scoped I/O, a suggestion is not a project and has no handle to take, and the scan asks about cc's own machine by construction (the dialog never names a system, and a remote adopt's probes already run on the named system inside `adoptProject`). Same standing as `listProjects`, which readdirs the store with `node:fs`.

- **src/projectConventions.ts** — catalog of optional CLAUDE.md conventions selectable at project creation (formerly "optional guidelines"), built on the shared `fragmentCatalog.ts`. `SEED_PROJECT_CONVENTIONS` (the built-in read-only conventions) is `{slug,name,description}` metadata only; bodies live in committed fragments `conventions/project/<slug>.md`. Custom conventions are persisted at `<store>/conventions/project.json` (`{rules:[…]}`; migration `0011` renamed the store from `optional-guidelines.json`, `0020` relocated it under `conventions/`). `getCatalog()` returns seeds + custom merged, each with `builtin:bool` and its body; `addCustomConvention`/`updateCustomConvention`/`deleteCustomConvention` for CRUD (seeds reject update/delete with 400; each custom mutation fans out via `regenerateAllProjectConventions()` — see `src/projectClaudeMd.ts`); `composeProjectConventionsBlock(slugs)` resolves slug list → markdown bodies (unknown slug → 400; a convention with no fragment body — scaffold-only — contributes nothing). A new project no longer inlines this block into `CLAUDE.md`; instead it imports a regenerated in-project `CONVENTIONS.md` (`src/projectClaudeMd.ts`). Imported by both `src/routes.ts` and `src/mcp/handlers.ts` (shared service layer); `src/projects.ts` stays circular-dep-free by accepting the pre-composed `CONVENTIONS.md` document as `{ conventionsDoc }` rather than importing projectConventions directly. **Plugin conventions** are folded in via `setPluginConventionsProvider(fn)` (a module-level setter `server.ts` wires to `pluginHost.conventions().project` after construction — avoids an import cycle; default no-op so plugin-less imports/tests work) passed as the catalog `extraProvider`; a plugin entry carries `body` (fragment, `''` when absent) and optional `scaffold` (resolved directive text). **Scaffolds are an optional plugin-only facet of a convention**, not a separate concept: `composeProjectScaffold(projectName, slugs)` (same module) resolves the picked slugs in selection order → one framed orchestrator-guidance block from those that carry a `scaffold` facet (unknown slug → 400; none → `''`). Scaffolds are **not persisted** — `create_project`/`POST /api/projects` RETURN the composed block under a `scaffold` field for the conductor to fold into the first worker brief (nothing touches the spawn path). Builtin seeds and custom conventions are fragment-only.
- **src/workspaceConventions.ts** — the toggleable workspace conventions, built on `fragmentCatalog.ts` and mirroring `conductorConventions.ts`. Always-on `conventions/workspace/core.md` (intro + the per-project `CONVENTIONS.md` delivery contract) precedes the enabled conventions. The composed text has **no destination of its own**: `src/projectClaudeMd.ts` folds it into every project's in-tree `CONVENTIONS.md` and `src/conduct.ts` prepends it to the role doc in `.conduct/CONVENTIONS.md`. `SEED_CONVENTIONS` (the built-in `{slug,name,description}` entries; bodies in `conventions/workspace/<slug>.md`) + custom conventions + the **global** selection persist at `<store>/conventions/workspace.json` (`{disabled:[slugs], rules:[…]}`). Every built-in and custom convention the `disabled` deny-list does not name is enabled — one rule, with no special case for an absent store or an absent key, so a fresh install reproduces the prior bundled canonical byte-for-byte and so does an install that has saved its settings. `getSelection`/`setSelection`/`deleteCustomConvention` come from the shared `src/conventionSelection.ts` collaborator with **no** hooks — the persisted `disabled` array is the whole selection state. Also `add/updateCustomConvention`, `composeWorkspace(slugs)`/`composeCurrentWorkspace()`. Imported by `src/routes.ts`, `src/projectClaudeMd.ts` and `src/conduct.ts`.
- **src/whisperModels.ts** — curated model catalog (`WHISPER_MODELS`, `DEFAULT_MODEL`), single source of truth for the picker + the install/switch allow-list (`isKnownModel`, `modelFileName`).
- **src/ollamaCloudModels.ts** (scoping rationale: [docs/models.md](models.md)) — a curated read-only catalog of Ollama cloud coding models (`OLLAMA_CLOUD_MODELS`, each `{model,label,contextWindow}`; tags verbatim from ollama.com, so `mistral-large-3` stays size-pinned) + `OLLAMA_CLOUD_TIER_DEFAULTS` (a per-tier UI pre-selection only) + `isKnownOllamaCloudModel`. Never persisted; **scoped to the built-in `ollama` backend row** (`isKnownBackendModel` accepts a preset only there) and a `contextWindowForModel` source. There is no per-backend health check or model catalog beyond this — a bad backend/model fails at spawn and surfaces as `launch_failed`.
- **src/effortLevels.ts** — the reasoning-effort vocabulary (`EFFORT_LEVELS`, `DEFAULT_EFFORT`, `INHERIT_EFFORT`, `isKnownEffort`), shipped to the client as the payload's `efforts`. Its OWN module, not part of `modelVersions.ts`: effort and the capability tiers are deliberately distinct vocabularies, and separate modules are what keep the two word-sets from bleeding together. Pure data — the per-tier/per-role storage and the precedence chain live in `appSettings.ts`.
- **src/modelVersions.ts** — curated per-family Claude model-version catalog (`MODEL_FAMILIES` (the family catalog), `DEFAULT_VERSIONS`) — this is the `claude` BACKEND's model list, shipped as the `claudeFamilies` payload key (`routes.ts` `modelsSettingsState`); the backend catalog proper is the registry (`models.backends`, see `docs/models.md`) — single source of truth for the Settings → Models Claude-version picker + the allow-list (`isKnownFamily`, `isKnownVersion`, `isKnownClaudeModel`, `defaultVersion`). Also exports `MANAGED_BACKENDS` / `MANAGED_BACKEND_IDS` (the code-owned managed registry rows) and `CLAUDE_BACKEND_ID` (the identity backend every "is this a substitution backend?" test compares against). Owns Claude context-window policy: each catalog version declares one numeric `contextWindow` plus an optional `launchTag` (the suffix the CLI needs to reach it, for a model whose native window ships as a separate build id). `claudeContextWindowTokens(id)` reads that number. `canonicalizeModel(id, backend)` applies the tag — `backend` is a **required positional**, and that gate is the only thing making it a no-op for a substitution model: any non-`claude` backend returns the id byte-exact, because that string is the registry key. Do **not** re-derive the gate from `familyOf(id)`: that is a naming heuristic, and inferring "not Claude ⇒ safe to normalize" from it is precisely what truncates a tagged substitution id and desynchronises it from its own registry row. Applied server-side at spawn/resume only — `public/models.js` holds no mirror.
  - **Capability tiers** (policy + the full binding contract: [docs/models.md](models.md)) — `CAPABILITY_TIERS` (the data-driven tier array in `src/modelVersions.ts`) + `DEFAULT_TIER_BACKEND` (each tier → `{backend:'claude', model:<family default version>}`) + `isKnownTier`. A tier binds to a `{backend, model}` pair via `getTierBackend`/`setTierBackend` (appSettings.ts), rebindable in Settings → Models. Resolution (in `spawnInstance`, `src/mcp/handlers.ts`): a tier → `getTierBackend(tier)` `{backend, model}` → passed to `create()` as `model` + `backend`. A **role** (built-in / user-custom / plugin-owned, checked via `isResolvableRole`) → `resolveRoleBackend(role)` — tiers and roles are a disjoint name-space (custom names can't be tier/family aliases; plugin names are `/`-namespaced), so the tier→role order is safe. A **legacy family alias** (`opus`/`sonnet`/`haiku`/`fable`) → `{backend:'claude', model:defaultVersion(family)}`; a **model id served by a configured backend** passed directly → that backend (`backendForModel`); a **Claude model id** → pass-through claude; **omitted entirely on a FRESH spawn** (no `resume`) → `defaultSpawnBinding()`, which also sets `tier` so that row's default effort applies; anything else is refused (no silent bare-claude spawn). The omitted branch is gated on `!resume`, and on a resume with no model named `resolveSpawnModel` resolves **neither** axis — it returns no backend at all (`backend: undefined`), deferring model+backend to `_doCreate`'s sidecar recovery; asserting `'claude'` there would make the resume's backend look explicit and suppress that recovery. The branch lives *downstream* of the MCP playbook gate — a stage `pin: {model}` has already filled `args.model` by then, so a pin always wins and never hits `ARG_PIN_CONFLICT`. The client mirror `public/models.js` `resolveSpawnModel(tier)` returns `{model, backend}` — the binding's ids verbatim, no launch tag applied (that is catalog policy, owned server-side by `canonicalizeModel`, which sees the authoritative `backend`). `backend` is the sole discriminator; `model` is a plain id for every backend, so resume/canonicalize apply uniformly.
- **src/installRunner.ts** — `makeInstallRunner(config)`, the shared factory behind `whisperInstall.ts` + `ttsInstall.ts`. Owns all the install-runner mechanics once: a process-wide single-run lock, a bounded in-memory log ring for progress polling, and persist-the-active-item on clean exit (`code 0`). Per-feature differences (script path + env var, allow-list `validate`, `persist` setter, nouns) are injected as config; returns the `{start, status, isRunning, _reset}` surface both modules re-export.
- **src/whisperInstall.ts** — whisper-specific config + re-exports over `installRunner.ts` (`bin/install-whisper.sh`, `WHISPER_INSTALL_SCRIPT` override, `isKnownModel` allow-list, `setTranscribeModel` persist). Sets the active model on clean exit.
- **src/tts.ts** — `isAvailable()` (piper venv python + active voice `.onnx`/`.onnx.json` on disk?) and `synthesize(text, {voice, rate})`. Spawns the venv python running `bin/piper-synth.py` (overridable via `PIPER_SYNTH_SCRIPT`), which loads the voice once and streams one self-contained WAV per sentence (`[4-byte LE length][WAV]` framing) to stdout — returned as a child process so the route pipes it straight to the HTTP response. Voice resolution: `PIPER_VOICE` env → Settings choice (`settings.json`) → built-in default; python from `PIPER_PYTHON`/`PIPER_VENV`; install root from `INSTALL_ROOT` (defaults to the central store `<store>`). `rate` maps to Piper's `length_scale` (inverse of speed). Build internals: `bin/install-piper.sh` `pkg install`s `onnxruntime python-onnxruntime espeak clang` and builds the venv under `<store>/piper/` (incl. a natively-compiled `espeakbridge.so` patched for Termux espeak-ng 1.52.0), downloading the `PIPER_VOICE_NAME` voice (default `en_US-lessac-medium`).
- **bin/piper-synth.py** — the streaming synthesizer `tts.ts` drives: reads text on stdin, iterates `PiperVoice.synthesize()` (one `AudioChunk` per sentence), wraps each chunk's PCM in a WAV via the stdlib `wave` module, and writes length-prefixed frames to stdout, flushing per sentence.
- **src/ttsModels.ts** — curated Piper voice catalog (`TTS_VOICES`, `DEFAULT_VOICE`), single source of truth for the picker + the install/switch allow-list (`isKnownVoice`, `voiceFileName`). Each entry carries its HF `rhasspy/piper-voices` subdir so the installer needn't parse names.
- **src/ttsInstall.ts** — TTS-specific config + re-exports over `installRunner.ts` (`bin/install-piper.sh`, `PIPER_INSTALL_SCRIPT` override, `isKnownVoice` allow-list, `setTtsVoice` persist). Sets the active voice on clean exit.
- **src/projectClaudeMd.ts** — regenerator of **every** project's in-tree `CONVENTIONS.md` (imported by its `CLAUDE.md` via `@CONVENTIONS.md`). The file carries **both** convention scopes, in this order: line-1 marker, blank line, the composed **workspace** conventions (`composeCurrentWorkspace()`, unconditional — the installation-wide selection, which deliberately does not travel with the project), then, only when there is something to put there, an H1 `# Project conventions` separator (`PROJECT_HEADING`; workspace sections are H2s under an H1, so without it project fragments would read as workspace ones) followed by the note and/or the project bodies. The **project** selection travels with the project: line 1 is a marker `<!-- cc:conventions <slug,…> -->` (built/parsed by `buildMarker`/`parseMarker`) so any cc instance regenerates that part from the file alone (survives move/rename/import into a store with no record of it, and does not require the project to sit under the projects root). `composeProjectConventionsDoc(slugs, missing?)` composes the whole document (its sibling `composeProjectConventionsDocWithMeta` returns `{text, degraded}` off the same catalog read, for the create path that warns on a degrade; the regeneration path below deliberately takes the plain form and stays silent, since it reaches this composition once per project per sweep); a visible in-body note (`` > Convention unavailable: `slug`[, `slug`…]. `` — `unresolvedNote`) names any `missing` slug in place of its body. Zero project slugs ⇒ marker + workspace text and nothing else (no heading, no note). `ensureProjectConventionsMd(name,{log})` reads the target (**ENOENT is not a skip** — it means "no project selection recorded", same as a non-marker line 1 and a zero-slug marker; all three regenerate with an empty project part and the old body discarded), fetches the catalog (`getCatalog()`, returning a `CatalogList` — see `fragmentCatalog.ts`) and resolves each marker slug against it: **resolvable slugs recompose normally**; an **unresolvable** slug (custom convention absent here, disabled/absent plugin, retired seed) **stays in the marker** — so it recovers verbatim if it resolves again — but is named in the note instead of contributing a body. It also calls `ensureConventionsImport(proj.path)` (`src/conventionsImport.ts`, shared with `src/conduct.ts`) on every non-declining run, so a project whose `CLAUDE.md` never had the import gains it — a `CONVENTIONS.md` nothing imports delivers nothing. The write is **declined, leaving the committed file untouched**, in exactly **one** case: ≥1 slug is unresolvable AND the catalog is `degraded` (its `extraProvider` threw outright, a plugin's own **placement** resolution transiently failed, or the last **discovery scan** could not resolve a project an enabled plugin lives in, so its manifest was never read and the plugin is absent from the catalog entirely — that third source is latched to the scan and stands until the next Rescan (or until the affected plugin is disabled), and it is a deliberate **superset** of the second: with the manifest unread, an enabled record is the only term left to flag on, so it flags even for a plugin that would have turned out to contribute no conventions at all (the compose-vs-scan split is tabulated in [plugins.md](plugins.md) → *Discovery rules*) — any of the three being indistinguishable from "genuinely gone", so declining avoids mistaking a hiccup for a removal and stripping real text; a plugin whose project cc no longer has any record of is NOT this case — that absence is confirmed, so those projects regenerate — see [plugins.md](plugins.md) → *Discovery rules* for the full split) → `{skipped:'catalog-degraded', missing}`. That freeze is **whole-file**, so the workspace region stays stale for that cycle too — bounded, for the first two sources, by the transience of the degradation, and for the scan-sourced one by the Rescan (or plugin disable) that ends it rather than by any transience; logged either way. The condition is unreachable for a project with no project slugs (`missing` is empty), and the **workspace** catalog carries no `extraProvider` at all (`src/workspaceConventions.ts`), so degradation can never leave a project with no conventions file. There is no `unresolvable` or `no-body` decline: both were permanent conditions whose only purpose was protecting committed text, and freezing them would permanently starve that project of workspace-convention updates. **No sentinel/backup/hand-edit detection**: the file is cc-owned and overwritten, whatever is in it; recovery for a hand-authored body is git (every project is a repo from birth, and the file is tracked). Because only line 1 is ever parsed — the previous body (note included) is discarded wholesale before recomposition — the document is a **pure function of (marker slugs, workspace selection, project catalog)**: repeated regeneration is byte-identical, neither the note nor the workspace block can accumulate, and the note vanishes cleanly (real text back, byte-identical) the moment the slug resolves again. **`ensureProjectConventionsMd` itself** (not the fan-out below) emits the three per-project log lines: `"CONVENTIONS.md left as-is for '<project>': catalog degraded, unresolvable <slugs>"` (catalog-degraded), `"CONVENTIONS.md regenerated without unresolvable <slugs>: <path>"` (partial resolution), and `"CONVENTIONS.md regenerated: <path>"` (full resolution). **Boot ordering is a real constraint on this one call, and its violation is silent.** `regenerateAllProjectConventions` composes the PROJECT catalog — the one carrying an `extraProvider` — so it must run *after* `createServer()` has wired `setPluginConventionsProvider`. Unwired, that provider is a no-op stub returning `[]`, every plugin-namespaced slug lands in `missing` and is filtered out *before* compose, and each project's committed `CONVENTIONS.md` is rewritten with the plugin's real text replaced by the `> Convention unavailable:` note. Nothing throws and the boot log is clean. Pinned by `tests/boot-plugin-conventions-order.test.mjs` (a subprocess boot of the real `server.ts` — the only shape that can fail when the statement order moves). Only the **project** part is at risk: the workspace block in the same document composes from the workspace catalog, which passes no `extraProvider` at all. `regenerateAllProjectConventions({log})` fans out over `listProjects()` (per-project try/catch) and owns exactly one line of its own — the only one signalling an actual fault rather than a designed decline — on a per-project exception: `"CONVENTIONS.md regenerate failed for '<project>': <message>"`. Called on boot (`server.ts`, non-fatal, after `ensureConductProject`) and after every Settings → Conventions → **Workspace** *and* → **Project** mutation (`src/routes.ts`, all seven routes passing `{log:console}`).
- **src/conventionsImport.ts** — the one implementation of "make this dir's `CLAUDE.md` carry a line whose `trim()` is exactly `@CONVENTIONS.md`" (`CONVENTIONS_IMPORT_LINE`, `ensureConventionsImport(dir)`). Absent ⇒ create with the import alone (`wx` flag); present without it ⇒ **prepend**, every existing byte kept below; present with it ⇒ **no write at all** (no mtime churn on repeat ensures). Detection is **line-level, not substring**, so prose mentioning the filename never reads as an import. Called by `ensureConductProject` (`src/conduct.ts`) and by `ensureProjectConventionsMd` (`src/projectClaudeMd.ts`); `migrations/0031-conduct-conventions-import.mjs` duplicates the literal and the prepend shape (built-ins only).
- **src/wsHub.ts** — Per-socket subscriptions, tail-only snapshot on subscribe (`snapshotTail()` + `tailStartSeq`/`trimmedBefore` frame metadata), fan-out, `prompt/mode/interrupt/kill/hook_decision/auto_approve_plan` over WS, `turn_notification` broadcast to all clients.
- **src/accountUsage.ts** — `getAccountUsage()`: fetches account-level usage from the Anthropic OAuth endpoint (token read from `~/.claude/.credentials.json`), a bounded cache (shorter cadence after a failed fetch), returns `null` on any error (never throws, never exposes the token). **Both** failure branches — a non-OK HTTP status and a network error/timeout — `console.warn` the reason plus the backoff they just armed (`Next retry in Ns (failure #N)`); a silent backoff on the network branch makes a persistently unreachable endpoint indistinguishable from "no overage to report" (the chip simply stays hidden). Strict fresh-or-null by default; an opt-in `{ allowStale, maxStaleMs}` (bounded max-staleness cap) serves the last retained payload — wrapped as `{ data, stale, fetchedAt }` — when a fresh fetch isn't available, used only by `GET /api/usage` so the chip doesn't blank on a transient 429/backoff. The overage-trip monitor and auto-resume controller call it with no options, so they always see strict fresh-or-null.
- **src/costTracking.ts** — per-turn cost persistence. `initCostTracking(instances)` subscribes to instance events and appends one JSONL row per `turn_end` to `<store>/costs.jsonl` (append-only). The `'event'` listener is synchronous and so cannot await the write: `appendCostRow` is itself **synchronous** — it snapshots the row (incl. `ts`, the live `inst`'s `project`/`model`/`sessionId`, and the resolved `costsPath()`) in the emit tick and hands the write to `writeCostRow` via a module-level promise chain (`serialize`, the same idiom as `tempSessions.ts`/`archivedSessions.ts`). Without the chain, two `turn_end`s in one tick start two independent `mkdir`→`appendFile` chains whose threadpool completion order is undefined, so rows land in arbitrary order (and two concurrent recursive `mkdir`s race, where a throw is swallowed into a `console.warn` — a dropped row); with it, file order == emit order. `appendCostRow` omits the row's `cost_usd` field entirely when `inst.backend !== 'claude'` (the CLI's `total_cost_usd` is Anthropic list pricing, meaningless against another provider's model). It likewise omits the four token fields (`input_tokens`/`output_tokens`/`cache_creation_tokens`/`cache_read_tokens`) whenever `ev.usage` is null — the case for every turn on a substitution backend (its `result` frames carry no `usage`, and `message_start` is a non-summable context snapshot, so there is no per-turn token total; writing 0 would fabricate data). The gate is `usage`-presence (mirrors the client `UsageTracker`), so Anthropic rows — which always carry `usage` — are byte-identical, and a conditional spread keeps the fields in-position. `getCostSummary()` reads it back into the `{total_usd, row_count, by_project[], by_model[], daily_trend[]}` aggregate behind `GET /api/costs/summary`; it counts a row's tokens only when `'cost_usd' in r` (the Anthropic marker, robust to legacy non-Claude rows with stale 0s) and emits `tokens_known` per `by_model`/`by_project` entry (false ⇒ the `#costs` dashboard shows `—` instead of a bogus 0). `costsPath()` exposes the file path. Each `by_model[]` entry and each `by_project[]` entry (plus its nested `by_project[].by_model[]`) carries a `cache_misses` field — a plain count of that model's/project's rows with `cache_miss === true` — and a `sessions` field — the count of distinct `sessionId`s among that model's/project's rows (rows with `sessionId == null` excluded) — both folded into the same reduce loop that builds cost/token totals (no separate aggregation pass). Cache-miss detection is **cross-turn**, captured live in `src/instances.ts`, and catches *partial* (minority) evictions a stateless single-request rule cannot. Each turn is one or more API requests; `message_start` carries per-request cumulative usage and `cache_read` accumulates across a turn's tool-call iterations (there is no per-iteration array — `parser.ts` passes `result.usage` through verbatim). So the detector uses two points: `read_N` = this turn's **first**-request `cache_read` (`_turnFirstReqCacheRead`, pre-tool-call); `P` = the **previous** turn's **last**-request full prefix `cache_read + cache_creation` (`_prevTurnPrefix`), captured by overwriting `_turnLastReqPrefix` on every `message_start` and latching it at `turn_end`. **Miss ⇔ `read_N < P − max(1024, 1% of P)`** — this turn served less of the prefix than was cached at the end of last turn ⇒ eviction (full or partial); a warm continuation reads exactly `P` (drop 0, never flagged), the tolerance absorbs tokenization-boundary noise. **Turn 1** (`_prevTurnPrefix === null`) and any turn where `_prefixBaselineInvalid` is set fall back to the stateless `creation > read` rule, which still flags a genuinely cold prefix (cold start, expiry, resume, cold rewind). **Guards** against legitimate prefix shrink: compaction (`system`/`compacting` in `_handleStdoutLine`), model switch (`_trackModel`'s real-switch branch — cache is model-specific), and rewind/respawn (`_wipeForResume`) each set `_prefixBaselineInvalid`; the next turn's first request consumes it (forces the fallback, re-establishes `P` fresh) so the cross-turn rule never false-fires. **Known limitation:** a miss on a request *after* the first (e.g. a turn outliving the ~1h cache TTL) isn't caught — first-request-only. Per-turn capture (`_turnFirstReqCacheRead`/`Creation`/`_turnMissDetected`/`_turnLastReqPrefix`/`_turnEvicted`) resets in `_setStatus`'s into-`turn` branch and on (re)spawn; cross-turn state (`_prevTurnPrefix`, `_prefixBaselineInvalid`) intentionally persists across the turn boundary. Each `turn_end` is enriched with `cacheMiss`/`firstReqCacheRead`/`firstReqCacheCreation`/`firstReqEvicted` (`P − read_N` on the cross-turn path), persisted as `cache_miss`/`first_req_cache_read`/`first_req_cache_creation`/`first_req_evicted` (additive), and an informational `system`/`cache_miss` notice fires once per miss turn. The old read-time session-relative heuristic (`cache_creation_tokens >= max(50000, 4 × session median)`) was retired to a one-shot backfill, **migration 0014**, which stamps `cache_miss` on legacy rows; a row still carrying the old `cache_flush` key (a decisive verdict captured before the field was renamed) is renamed in place rather than recomputed, so historical rows are heuristic-or-renamed while new rows are decisive. Migration 0014 additionally stamps `cache_miss_heuristic: true` on any row whose verdict IT decided (neither key present, or a renamed `cache_flush` row lacking `first_req_cache_read` — an old-migration guess, not a live capture); presence is checked, not truthiness, since a decisive row can legitimately have `first_req_cache_read: 0`. `appendCostRow` never sets this field, so its absence always means "decisive."
- **src/projectsCache.ts** — short-TTL (default `PRODUCTION_TTL_MS` in `src/projectsCache.ts`) cache of per-project git facts with in-flight coalescing, to cut the `GET /api/projects` fan-out load. `getOrCompute()`/`invalidate()`/`invalidateAll()`, plus `projectCacheKey(systemId, name)` — the key is `<systemId>:<name>` and the composer is exported so key and invalidation cannot drift. The system is not there to disambiguate (names are globally unique): the FACTS are measured on that system, and an entry keyed on the name alone would survive a placement change and be served as facts about the machine the project left. `invalidate()` correspondingly takes the PROJECT NAME and clears every key for it — its callers are mutations that know which project changed and not which system it is on, and a placement change is itself a reason to invalidate.
- **src/health.ts** — boot readiness probe (`checkClaudeReadiness`/`formatReadiness`): `claude --version` (timeout `DEFAULT_TIMEOUT_MS` in `src/health.ts`) + credential check; returns the framed warning banner server.ts prints to stderr. Non-fatal.
- **src/mcp/** — `server.ts` (JSON-RPC 2.0 over Streamable HTTP), `tools.ts` (static registry), `handlers.ts` (thin shells over InstanceManager + projects + worktrees — never duplicate business logic, never self-HTTP). The handler shell delegates its bulky-output shaping to five siblings so the documented MCP output shapes stay identical: `content.ts` (`textPayload()` — builds the raw, un-escaped body blocks for text-payload tools; `flattenPayload(meta, bodies)` — joins them into the single string the idle wake-callback folds inline; `textResult(text)` — the rendered read tools' channel, a single plain-text block and no JSON), `textRender.ts` (tool-agnostic presentation primitives — `table`/`ts`/`bytes`/`trunc`/`block` plus `deviations(row, spec)`, the declarative engine that surfaces a field only when it leaves its declared default), `readRenderers.ts` (the per-tool renderers over those primitives — the recon read tools plus `renderPlaybook` for `describe_playbook`; its header comment records which payload fields are deliberately dropped and why, and why `renderPlaybook` must not be merged with `renderPlaybookConvention` — see [protocol.md → Rendered read results](protocol.md#rendered-read-results)), `diffPaging.ts` (`indexDiffLines`/`paginateDiff` — the pure `project_diff` byte-bounded pagination engine; the numstat/name-status parsing lives in `../gitDiff.ts`, shared with the REST diff surface), `messageReconstruction.ts` (`reconstructMessages`/`mergeRecentWithDisk`/`loadDiskSelection`/`capText`/`capBlockInput`/`hasPlanOrQuestions`/`ringTurnIndex`/`diskTurnIndex`/`bondTrailingTurn` + `MSG_TEXT_CAP`/`TOOL_ARG_VALUE_CAP` — `get_recent_messages` assembly, ring↔disk merge, and turn-scoped default-call bonding; `capBlockInput(block, verbatim)` takes the caller's `includeToolCalls` — false renders a per-argument descriptor, true the whole input under `MSG_TEXT_CAP` as before). `bondTrailingTurn` takes a `TurnIndex` whose seq list is named `turnBoundarySeqs`, not `turnEndSeqs`: `ringTurnIndex` fills it from `turn_end` events, `diskTurnIndex` from outer `user_echo` events, and the two partition a message list identically — which is what lets bonding work on a replayed transcript at all, since the CLI never persists `turn_end`. `loadDiskSelection` is the instance-free selection for a retired session (replay → stamp → tail-cap → `reconstructMessages` + `diskTurnIndex`), applying `DISK_REPLAY_TAIL_CAP` **after** stamping so the surviving seqs keep their original dense values. `get_recent_messages`'s SELECTION (resolve, reconstruct, bond) is factored into a module-private `selectRecentMessages(args, ctx)` — which resolves through `getInstOrDisk`, the read-only sibling of `getInst` (live instance → disk location → soft refusal), and so returns `{sessionId, trimmedBefore}` rather than an `InstanceLike` there may be none of. It feeds **two** renderers off the same selection: the exported `buildRecentMessages(args, ctx)` returning `{meta, bodies}` (or `{soft}`) — `getRecentMessages` wraps it in `textPayload`, and `idleSubscriptions.ts` reuses it to fold the identical content into the wake stub without drift — and `send_prompt`'s worker-facing `renderForwardFrame`, a deliberately different, telemetry-stripped view over the same default selection (see [protocol.md → `send_prompt({forward})`](protocol.md#mcp-tool-protocol)). Still five siblings: both live in `handlers.ts` alongside `renderMessageBody`/`messageBoundaryHeader`/`renderQuestions`, not a sixth file.
- **public/** — `index.html`, `styles.css`, and a set of ES modules (no build step). `app.js` (~800 lines) is **bootstrap + reactive `state` + wiring only**: it builds `state`/the DOM map, calls each module's `installX({…getters,…callbacks})` factory once, holds the returned handles, and wires the WS router **last**. Feature logic lives in the modules, which read live state through injected getters (`getActiveId: () => state.activeId`) rather than touching globals (see Conventions → frontend module pattern). Topology:
  ```
  app.js   bootstrap + reactive `state` + DOM map; wires everything, owns no feature logic
    │  installX({ getters, callbacks }) — each module installed once at init
    ├─ dialogs ......... newProjectDialog · workspaceDialog · spawnDialog
    ├─ flows/actions ... restartFlow · sessionActions · lazyHistory (controller)
    ├─ chrome .......... sidebarChrome (drawer/scrim · resize · ≡ overflow)
    ├─ header .......... header (active-instance chips + usage popover + controls)
    ├─ polling ......... accountUsage (/api/usage → globalRLTracker)
    ├─ hash views ...... settings · review · commits · costs   (via hashView scaffold)
    └─ wsRouter ........ installed LAST; routes `bus` events into the modules below
         shared leaf modules (imported where needed, not app-wired):
         conversation · blocks · diff · markdown · dom · http · models · usage ·
         tts · tts-queue · md-to-speech · sidebar · composer · tasks · subagents ·
         notifications · anchor · dismissable · lightbox · external-links · ws ·
         unread
  ```
  - **Wiring hub & WS** — `app.js`; `ws.js` (reconnecting + ack-based `bus`); `wsRouter.js` (`installWsRouter({…})` — the bus data/routing handlers `snapshot`/`reset_snapshot`/`event`/`turn_notification`/`status`/`instances`/`projects`, window `popstate`, and the first-connect anchor-restore/auto-resume; installed after `installRestart` so restart's `open` listener registers first). What `app.js` still owns is only wiring: `state` + the `dom` map, the per-instance tracker maps (`getTracker`/`getUsage`) and `globalRLTracker`, `sendCardAnswer` + `conversationOptions`, the three orchestration functions (`refreshProjects`/`refreshInstances`/`selectInstance`) injected into nearly every module, and a handful of 3–4-line callbacks passed into an `installX` (`closeSettings`, `closeReview`, `setSidebarStatus`, `setMicAvailable`, `getActiveSid`). Its `installSessionActions` call sits **above** `installHeader` because the header's control handlers take `sessionActions` as a dependency.
  - **Sidebar chrome & unread** — `sidebarChrome.js` (`installSidebarChrome({dom})` → `{setSidebarOpen, closeSidebarOnMobile, closeSidebarOverflow}` — the mobile drawer + scrim, the desktop drag-resize handle and its `code-conductor:sidebar-width` clamp (`clampSidebarWidth`, 220–560 px), and the sidebar `≡` overflow menu; installed **immediately after the `dom` map** because `closeSidebarOverflow` is passed by value into `installNewProjectDialog`/`installSpawnDialog`), `unread.js` (`createUnreadStore({onChange})` → `{counts, bump, clear}` — the per-`sessionId` unread counts and their `code-conductor:unread` localStorage persistence; `counts` is the live `Map` the sidebar renders from, and an empty map `removeItem`s rather than storing `{}`).
  - **Account usage** — `accountUsage.js` (`installAccountUsage({globalRLTracker, getActiveId, headerUpdate})` → `{refresh, get, isStale}` — the 180 s `/api/usage` poll; merges the first present `RL_BUCKET_KEYS` bucket into `globalRLTracker` as a synthetic `rate_limit_event`, dividing `utilization` by 100 and converting `resets_at` to epoch seconds. A non-ok reply or a null `usage` keeps the last-good value rather than blanking the chip).
  - **Dialogs** — `newProjectDialog.js` (`installNewProjectDialog` — name→path preview + create), `workspaceDialog.js` (`installWorkspaceDialog` → `{openNew, openEdit}`), `spawnDialog.js` (`installSpawnDialog` → `{openSpawnDialog,…}` — new-session dialog + Conduct button handler + the three model pickers).
  - **Flows & session actions** — `restartFlow.js` (`installRestart` — restart / Resume-after-restart flow + poll-and-reload), `sessionActions.js` (`installSessionActions` — promote / resume / rewind / fork / delete-session / delete-project / remove-worktree handlers; fork/rewind composer prefill rides `droppedText` inline on the WS `snapshot`/`reset_snapshot` frame, applied in `wsRouter.js` — no client-side prefill state), `lazyHistory.js` (`installLazyHistoryController` — scroll-up `/events` paging via `renderEventBatch` through a fresh detached `Conversation`, then `spliceBatchAbove`: viewport-preserving prepend + **seam gap collapse** (a batch ending in a `history_gap` divider spliced above a chunk that begins with one drops the duplicate — the pager can mark one seam on two pages; adjacency-scoped, so gaps separated by content both stay) + **seam-bubble merge** (a batch ending with an open assistant segment splices its trailing blocks into the below chunk's leading bubble — tracked via `Conversation.leadingAssistantWrap`; `history_gap` and any user action are merge barriers) + **sub-agent adoption** (live child events whose parent Task head sat below the tail are parked per `parentToolUseId` in `Conversation.orphanChildEvents` instead of leaking to the outer level; when a page brings the head in, `adoptToolBlock` replays them in arrival order into its nested panel — reconstructing multi-part nested blocks whole — and future children route natively); an empty page no longer ends history on its own — `hasMore` also requires `nextBefore` to have strictly decreased from the cursor that produced it and a consecutive-empty-page streak under `MAX_EMPTY_PAGES` (module constant), so a served page resets the streak and a pathological server that hot-loops empty pages still terminates).
  - **Active-instance header** — `header.js` (`installHeader({…}) → {update}` — chip row + primary controls + combined ctx/rate-limit chip and its usage popover).
  - **Hash-routed full-page views** — `hashView.js` (`installHashView` — hash-route + escape/teardown scaffold shared by the next four), `settings.js` (`#settings` — Voice (Dictation + Speech) / Models / Archived / Workspace-conventions groups), `review.js` (`#review` diff renderer, `open({title,url,onBack})` — shared by the worktree `±` and per-commit diffs), `commits.js` (`#commits` history list + client-side branch/merge graph; rows delegate to `review.open`), `costs.js` (`installCosts` — `#costs` cost dashboard; fetches `GET /api/costs/summary`).
  - **Shared leaf helpers** — `dom.js` (`el(tag, attrs, ...children)` — the one DOM builder, imported by `blocks.js`, `sidebar.js`, `conversation.js` and `markdown.js`. It is a **leaf** because `blocks.js` imports `markdown.js`, so an `el` living in `blocks.js` would make `blocks → markdown → blocks` a cycle. `attrs` is mandatory in practice: a first argument that is a string or an element is read as an attribute bag and either sets numeric attributes (`0="t"`, `1="e"`, …) or drops the child silently — both pinned by `tests/markdown-attr-surface.test.mjs`), `http.js` (`apiFetch(url, opts)` — the client counterpart of `src/httpError.ts`; returns the parsed JSON body, throws ``Error(body.error || `HTTP <status>`)``, and never leaks a JSON `SyntaxError` from a non-JSON error body. Callers needing the raw `Response` — a status-specific branch, a body stream, an error carrying extra fields — keep their own `fetch`).
  - **Conversation rendering** — `conversation.js` (sticky-scroll, idempotent by `_seq`, routes by `parentToolUseId`, rewind/fork anchored on server-stamped `userIndex`), `blocks.js` (per-tool summaries + body renderers), `diff.js` (Myers' line-diff), `markdown.js` (safe Markdown → DOM, `textContent` only), `lightbox.js` (`installLightbox` — tap-to-zoom image overlay), `subagents.js` (`SubagentPanel` — conductor sub-agent strip), `tasks.js` (`TaskTracker`/`TaskPanel`).
  - **Voice / TTS** — `tts.js` (Piper playback: POSTs `/api/tts`, decodes the framed-WAV stream via Web Audio, holds availability/enabled/rate + first-gesture state), `tts-queue.js` (`TtsQueue` — gapless per-sentence FIFO), `md-to-speech.js` (`mdToSpeech` — strips markdown before synthesis).
  - **Models & usage** — `models.js` (client mirror of the server model catalog; `resolveSpawnModel(tier)` the three spawn pickers call — resolves a capability tier to its bound backend + version, fed by `src/modelVersions.ts` via `/api/settings/models`. Its pre-fetch fallbacks are **drift-tested** against `src/modelVersions.ts` + `src/effortLevels.ts` by `tests/models-fallback-drift.test.mjs` — all nine mirrors, keys and order included; `getEffortLevels()` is the one the ⋮ Change-effort picker renders), `usage.js` (`UsageTracker` / `RateLimitTracker`, the pure formatters `formatTokens`/`formatPct`/`formatDuration`/`fmtCost`, the three reset-time formatters, and the single ordered rate-limit bucket list `RL_BUCKET_KEYS` + its long-form `RL_WINDOW_LABEL`). `usage.js` imports nothing, so every consumer can reach it; `tests/usage-model-no-capacity.test.mjs` pins that `UsageTracker` derives no context capacity from the model it observed (capacity is the server's `contextWindowTokens`, always).
  - **Sidebar, composer & misc** — `sidebar.js` (Project ▸ Sessions ▸ Worktrees subnodes), `composer.js` (Enter / Shift+Enter, attachments, hold-to-dictate), `notifications.js` (Service-Worker OS pings), `anchor.js` (`#session=<sid>` helpers), `dismissable.js` (`makeDismissable` — click-outside/Esc factory), `external-links.js` (`installExternalLinkOpener` — Android `target=_blank` workaround), `sw.js` (Service Worker).
- **tests/** — `node:test` suite (see Testing below).
- **harness/playwright/** — Opt-in Playwright + Termux-Chromium harness (sibling-repo dep).
- **migrations/** — Idempotent on-disk migrations; see "Migrations" below.

## Conventions

Load-bearing rules this codebase follows — keep new work inside them so the project stays manageable as it grows.

1. **YAGNI — build for the requirement in front of you, not the one you imagine.** Implement only what a current, concrete caller or test needs; don't add speculative abstractions, config knobs, or extension points "for later" — they add surface area without a real user, and the eventual real requirement rarely matches the guess. If code stops being exercised by a real caller or test, delete it rather than keeping it "just in case" or "to trim later" — dead code rots silently and misleads the next reader into thinking it's load-bearing.
2. **Frontend: `installX({…})` modules; `app.js` is wiring-only.** Feature logic lives in a `public/` module exposing an `installX({…getters,…callbacks})` factory (or a class for a stateful widget). `app.js` only builds `state`/the DOM map, calls each `installX` once, holds the returned handles, and wires `wsRouter` last. Modules read live state through injected getters (`getActiveId: () => state.activeId`) — they never reach into globals. Don't grow `app.js` with feature logic; add a module and wire it.
3. **No god-modules — extract composed collaborators.** When a module takes on a second responsibility, lift it into a collaborator the owner *composes*, keeping the public surface stable via delegation. Cf. `InstanceManager` → `IdleSubscriptionHub` + `OverageResumeController`; `mcp/handlers.ts` → `diffPaging` + `messageReconstruction` + `content` + `textRender`/`readRenderers`; whisper/tts install → the `makeInstallRunner` factory; the convention scopes' catalog → `conventionSelection.ts`'s `createSelectionStore`.
4. **One service layer for REST + MCP — don't reimplement per surface.** Git / worktree / diff / session logic lives once in `src/worktrees.ts` (+ siblings) and is imported by **both** `src/routes.ts` and `src/mcp/handlers.ts` (shared: `isGitRepo`, `mergeWorktreeIntoParent`, `syncWorktree`, `getWorktree`, `resolveProjectCwd`, `getWorktreeMergeStatus`, `worktreeDirtyLines`, `removeWorktree`, …). A new git/worktree op goes in the service layer and is called from both surfaces — never duplicated.
5. **Single-source-of-truth catalogs, shipped to the client — never client literals.** Curated catalogs (`modelVersions.ts`, `whisperModels.ts`, `ttsModels.ts`) own the authoritative list + allow-list + policy server-side; the client *fetches* them (`/api/settings/models` → `public/models.js`) instead of hardcoding the canonical set. (`public/models.js` keeps a `DEFAULT_VERSIONS` first-paint fallback — a fallback, not a second source of truth. `tests/models-fallback-drift.test.mjs` is what keeps that fallback honest: it asserts every one of its nine mirrors still equals the server value it mirrors, so a catalog change that forgets the client fails the suite instead of silently showing a stale default until the boot fetch lands.)
6. **Cross-surface magic strings get one owner module.** A canonical string shared across surfaces lives in a single module. Cf. `planApproval.ts` — the approve/reject prompts shared by the UI buttons, the server-side auto-approve, and the MCP `approve_plan`/`reject_plan` tools; `public/userQuestionAnswers.js` — the AskUserQuestion answer formatter shared by the UI question card and the MCP `answer_question` tool (a DOM-free module under `public/` that the server imports directly, avoiding a mirror); `public/wakeCallback.js` — the idle wake-stub marker (`WAKE_CALLBACK_MARKER`/`WAKE_BODY_SEP`) shared by the server (build) and the client (parse/render); `SOFT_INTERRUPT_MARKER` in `parser.ts`; `isTaskNotificationContent` and `isInterruptMarkerContent` in `parser.ts` — the CLI's re-injected `<task-notification>` tag and its post-abort `[Request interrupted by user]` line, both recognized by shape (no marker to inject, since the CLI produces the string) and checked at the same three sites as `SOFT_INTERRUPT_MARKER`. On the client the same rule covers the rate-limit vocabulary: the ordered bucket key list (`RL_BUCKET_KEYS`) and the long-form window label map (`RL_WINDOW_LABEL`) have exactly one home, `public/usage.js`, read by `accountUsage.js`'s fetch merge, `header.js`'s popover rows and `blocks.js`'s `rate_limit_event` lines. The order is load-bearing for both uses (which single window is most immediate, and the popover's row order), so a second list would let the chip and the popover disagree. The terse chip-only `RL_BUCKET_LABEL` stays private to `usage.js` — that long/terse split is deliberate, not drift. Likewise `el` (`dom.js`) and `apiFetch` (`http.js`) are the single owners of DOM construction and the fetch-error idiom.
7. **Test-only exports carry an explicit marker.** An export with no production caller that is kept for its tests opens its doc comment with `// Test-only export: no production caller.` followed by one line of *why it is kept rather than deleted with its tests*. Cf. `hasSessionBackend` (`sessionBackends.ts`), `snapStartToGroupBoundary` (`parser.ts`), `getSubstitutionBackends` (`appSettings.ts`), `isKnownVersion` (`modelVersions.ts`). Reset hooks (`_resetForTest`, `_resetCache`) are a distinct, self-naming category and need no marker. An export with **no** caller at all — production or test — is deleted, not marked.
8. **Project-scoped I/O goes through the project's `System` unless it is on the exception list below.** Anything that touches a project's tree — git, reading or writing a file in it, running a command in it — takes the handle `getProject`/`resolveProjectDir`/`resolveSystem` hands out (`src/systems/`). cc's OWN paths are the complement rather than exceptions: the store under `<projectsRoot>/.code-conductor/`, `~/.claude/**`, `<projectsRoot>/.plugins/` and the self-update checkout are local by definition and keep their direct calls. **Resolving a project is NOT project-scoped I/O** — it is one store read and touches no tree at all. **A worker's FUSE run directory is one of them** — it lives under the store, the mount scaffolding is the orchestrator's own, and `src/systems/fuse/` writes it with `node:fs` on purpose. A worker reads its project through the union at the project's real path on its system. 

   **The exception list — bare `fs`/`spawn` on a project path, sanctioned, with the reason each is sanctioned.** Adding to it is a design decision, not a shortcut; anything not on it is a leak.
   - **`src/plugins/supervisor.ts`'s backend spawn** (`_spawn('bash', ['-lc', manifest.backend.start], {cwd: <project dir>})`) — project-scoped and deliberately bare: plugin backends are **local-only**, because crossing needs remote process lifecycle plus a port forwarded back to cc. A remote-system project refuses the affordance instead.
   - **`Instance.launch`'s CLI spawn** (`instances.ts`, via the injected `ClaudeLauncher`) — cwd is the project tree, deliberately bare: the `claude` CLI always runs locally, whatever system the project lives on — a remote system cannot be assumed to host node or Claude credentials.
   - **The repo-tracked plugin surface's reads inside a project tree** — `readManifest` plus the declared-path `fs.access` checks beside it (`plugins/manifest.ts`: `<tree>/conductor.plugin.json` and the `conventions[].file` / `scaffold.file` it names), `worktreeManifestFallback` (`plugins/registry.ts`, which feeds a worktree's `worktreePath` into that), and `claudePluginDirs`' `fs.access` on `<cwd>/<rel>/.claude-plugin/plugin.json` (`plugins/contributions.ts`) — bare for the same reason as the backend spawn above: repo-tracked plugin contributions are **local-only**, and that member skips a non-`local` placement before it resolves anything. P4 owns their refusals. (`readFragment` in the same module is NOT on this list: a fragment body is read through the placement's `System` handle.)
   - **The Plugin Library's install-target paths** (`plugins/library.ts` — the installed-check `fs.stat`, the failed-clone `fs.rm` and the update precheck's `fs.stat`, all on `path.join(projectsRoot(), name)`) — cc-level, never per-project: the library only ever clones into the local projects root — the one marginal misreport being a library name colliding with a remote project's.
   - **`ensureConductProject`'s `fs.mkdir`** (`conduct.ts`) — `.conduct` is pinned to the local system unconditionally, so its dir is cc's own by invariant. The `CLAUDE.md` write beside it *does* take the resolved handle: that is the pin being used, not an inconsistency to tidy away.
   - **`listProjects`' enumeration of the projects root** (`projects.ts`) — the root is cc's own registry area, not a project tree. A remote project does not live in it; P4 adds remote records by unioning store-derived entries onto this local enumeration, so routing the `readdir` through a System would be the wrong fix.

   The rule is enforced for everything else by `tests/systems-seam.test.mjs`, which watches `fs.promises` and `node:child_process` while real operations run — against both an in-root and an adopted project — and fails on any call that reached a project tree without a `LocalSystem` frame beneath it. So a reintroduced direct call is a named failure, not a silent one that works until the project moves to another machine.

## Instance lifecycle
```
create → spawning → idle ←─ turn ─→ turn_end ─┐
            ↓        ↓                          ↓
        load-hist  prompt                  exited / crashed
        fails                                   │
                                                ▼ respawn --resume <sid>
                                            (back to spawning)
```

**Terminal latch (`Instance.spawn` → `_procEnded`).** Every launch wires a **one-shot** latch on the child's `'exit'` **AND** `'close'`, whichever arrives first; it resolves `Instance._procEnded` and calls `_handleExit` exactly once. `kill()` awaits `_procEnded` — never a freshly-registered `proc.once('exit')` — and clears its SIGTERM/SIGKILL timers in a `finally`, keyed on no event at all. Two shapes force this:
- **A child whose spawn FAILED emits `'error'` then `'close'` and NEVER `'exit'`** (measured: +10 ms / +14 ms, both long before anything reaps it). Keying the terminal path on `'exit'` alone left `proc` assigned with `proc.pid === undefined` — and `proc != null` is the liveness oracle (`liveForSession`/`isSessionLive`), so `kill`, `remove`, `shutdown`, `respawn`, the resume manifest and every cross-module `.proc` read saw the corpse as alive; `kill()` and `InstanceManager.shutdown()` never returned.
- **A healthy child killed while a detached grandchild holds its stdout pipes emits `'exit'` and no `'close'`** (measured: `exit(null,'SIGKILL')` at +417 ms, no `close` in the following 2.6 s) — so `'close'` cannot be the primary key either, and the latch must be order-agnostic.

`'error'` is deliberately **not** in the latch: it also fires post-spawn (a failed `kill`), so it is not terminal and never nulls `proc`. The latch is the only thing that does. Because the flag is per-launch closure state, a stale launch's late event can never reach a newer launch's handler — no `this.proc !== launched` identity check is needed. Pinned by `tests/spawn-failure-teardown.test.mjs` (both a real `child_process` spawn against a nonexistent `CLAUDE_BIN` and an injected launcher, which is what makes the mid-kill race window reachable).

On **resume**: `loadHistory(sessionId)` runs before flipping to `idle` — replays jsonl into UI events and emits a `system/history_replayed` divider. A history longer than the ring cap trims during the replay itself; the evicted prefix stays reachable via `GET /api/instances/:id/events`. On **turn end** or **mode change**: append `{"type":"last-prompt", …}` + `{"type":"permission-mode", …}` lines so `claude --resume`'s interactive picker can discover the session.

**Resume reclaims the instances it supersedes (`InstanceManager._doCreateResolved`).** A resume is guarded by `isSessionLive(publicId)` in `_doCreate` — not `liveForSession` — which **subsumes** the `liveForSession` + `_resumingPublicIds` pair it replaced and additionally refuses **409** inside a relaunch window (`rotationPending` / `_relaunching`: a prune's kill→relaunch, a rewind's, a respawn's), where a `proc`-only guard let a resume start a second CLI on one transcript. Past that guard, every in-memory instance answering to the session is a superseded husk, so each is retired via `InstanceManager.remove(id)`. The reclaim is the **last statement before `byId.set`**, not part of the guard: every refusal in `_doCreateResolved` — mode/thinking/effort validation, backend and worktree resolution, the mirror-geometry and hook/bash-rule checks, `FUSE_LAUNCHER_UNRESOLVED`, the resume pre-flight, `FUSE_BACKEND_UNRESOLVED` — runs above it and none is rolled back, so reclaiming earlier let a *refused* resume destroy the session's existing instance (`create({resume, thinking:'garbage'})` over a husk left the session with zero). The span from `new Instance(…)` to `byId.set` is pure listener wiring, no await and no throw, so nothing between the reclaim and **registration** can fail. That is the whole of the claim, and deliberately not a guarantee about the create as a whole: `launch()` runs **after** `byId.set` and carries its own refusals — `MIRROR_ADVERTISEMENT_CHANGED` (501) and `_assertRemoteMountable`'s `FUSE_REMOTE_UNREACHABLE` (502) / `FUSE_MIRROR_ROOT_ABSENT` (501). Those ask the system **live, per spawn**, precisely because the create-time mirror read is memoised off the connection generation and so cannot see a box that stopped since. Resuming a remote/FUSE session whose box is down therefore tears the husk down, registers the replacement, and only then refuses. **Known, and deliberately not fixed:** deferring the reclaim past `launch()` would leave the husk and its replacement co-registered for the entire launch, reopening the `anyForSession` first-match window this reclaim exists to close. A narrow error-path loss is the better side of that trade. Post-condition: at most one `byId` entry answers to any public sessionId, which is what makes `anyForSession`'s first-match unambiguous. Without it the husk stayed, `anyForSession` resolved **it** (insertion order), and its auto-resume deadline, idle-wake edges, renew arm, redirect connection and tmp root leaked — `remove()` is the one teardown that reclaims all five, and a stranded deadline holds the **global** overage lockout (`_maybeReleaseOverageLock` short-circuits while `_overageResume.timers` is non-empty). Same rule `detachInstancesForSession` (`src/routes.ts`) applies at the REST layer, moved to the chokepoint, so the sidebar one-click resume and the anchor auto-resume get it too: resuming a dead non-temp session now **replaces** its sidebar row instead of adding a second.
- **Safety, and its exact boundary.** `remove()` calls `kill()`, so the reclaim is sound only where nothing it reaches is live. At the guard that holds absolutely: `isSessionLive` false is a `.some()` over **every** answering instance, so all of them have `proc === null` and both relaunch flags clear, and `kill()` takes its already-settled early-out. But `await this.remove(staleId)` is a real event-loop window (a FUSE husk's `kill()` early-out still awaits `_fuse.teardown()`), so the question is what can make one live *during* it. Two of the three in-place revival paths are closed: a competing `create({resume})` is refused by the `_resumingPublicIds` claim, which is `isSessionLive`'s first line; and `InstanceManager.respawn` (`POST /api/instances/:id/respawn`, the UI Respawn button) now refuses **409** while that claim is held — synchronously, with no await between the check and its `_relaunching = true`, which also closes the reverse order through the guard. **The exception, stated rather than assumed:** `Instance.rewindToUserMessage` and `Instance.pruneSession`, reached from `POST /api/instances/:id/rewind` and `/prune`, resolve by **instanceId** and require no live process, so a rewind/prune landing inside the reclaim window can still have its fresh process killed. `Instance` holds no back-reference to the manager, so neither can consult the claim from where it runs. Pre-existing and not narrowed by the reclaim — before it, the same interleaving produced a *double-spawn* on one transcript instead — but it is the one case in which this bullet's guarantee does not hold. Closing it wants `_mutating` folded into `isLiveOrComingUp`, which widens the authority the playbook gate reads and is not this card's to take.
- **Race — both actors, not just the deleting one.** A *deleter* can land in the `await` gap: the temp-exit status listener also does `byId.delete`, from the subprocess exit handler. That reaches the same post-condition, so the reclaim treats **404 as success** and propagates every other error. A *reviver* can land there too, which is the previous bullet: 404-as-success says nothing about it, because a revived husk is still **present** in `byId` — it is refused at the entry point instead, never absorbed here.

Covered by `tests/mcp-resume-reclaim.test.mjs`.

**`displayStatus` overlay (additive, not a 6th state).** The diagram above is the real `status` enum — untouched.
